--- lineage:
Cell 20 References name these sources: (1) Briano, "Timeline + Graph", kaggle.com/code/woominyo/lb-0-890-wear-hasca-2026-timeline-graph, for timeline reconstruction and the graph. (2) Jiwei Liu, kaggle.com/code/jiweiliu/public-fast-gpu-inference, for window models, fast inference and Apache-2.0 weights. (4) goodpjw2008, kaggle.com/code/goodpjw2008/wear-hasca-learned-links-counts-lb-0-92942, for learned links and counts. The author claims these additions: query-grouped ranking with clean-margin confidence, consensus boundary features, fixed refiner-seed averaging, whitened graph geometry, and the cross-fitting audit. The experiment log says the timeline stage came from "Timeline reconstruction on the public stack (Jiwei Liu, Briano)" at 0.9077. --- compute:
kernel-metadata.json sets enable_gpu false, enable_tpu false and enable_internet false, so this is a CPU kernel. Cell 4 sets OMP_NUM_THREADS, OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS, MKL_NUM_THREADS, VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS and NUMEXPR_NUM_THREADS to "4". The LightGBM predict call uses num_threads=4. Runtime is printed through elapsed() in cells 12, 16 and 19, but no outputs were saved, so runtime and memory are unknown. The notebook does little heavy work. It only rebuilds the TEST-side graph (hp.bag_graph run twice, five configurations each, over 12,234 windows), fits two ridge count models, and trains four LightGBM refiners of 300 rounds each. All upstream models and the dev-side graph are loaded from npz files.

--- tile_construction:
This notebook does not build tiles, so none of it can be read here. Building windows, assigning limbs, choosing video frames, deciding the label rule and applying exclusions all happen upstream, in the precomputed npz files and/or hasca_pipeline.py. The only facts available are these. Cell 1 says "Development data | 69,326 windows from 22 participants, grouped into five participant folds" and "Test | 12,234 windows". Dev arrays are indexed by row of strict_oof_pregraph.npz and carry pre["subject"], pre["fold"] and pre["y"]. The code never shows a window start offset, stride, limb sampling seed, frame index, majority-vs-centre label rule, or a correction for sbj_10, sbj_2 or sbj_7. To find the recipe, list np.load(EVID+'/strict_oof_pregraph.npz').files (there may be unused keys) and read hasca_pipeline.py. Failing that, read the upstream Jiwei Liu or Briano notebooks.
--- cv_protocol:
Folds come from five participant folds, pre["fold"], loaded in cell 4. The variable folds is never used in this notebook. The dev-side graph outputs (graph["P0"], graph["P1"], graph["probabilities"]) are precomputed and described as "cross-fitted" in the cell 13 plot title.

Cell 20 describes the repaired protocol. The count models must exclude both the outer fold and each profile's own fold in both count passes. Before this fix, "Changing only the held-out fold's count targets moved 32 held-out predictions"; after it the result is "exactly invariant".

The refiner in cell 16 is trained on ALL dev proposals, with no held-out evaluation in this notebook. Seeds are hp.REFINER_SEEDS, which the markdown says are four fixed seeds; their values are not shown.

The author admits the dev estimate is "not a fully nested estimate" because upstream choices were made on the same folds. A change was accepted only with "a positive pooled change and at least four positive folds".

--- window_blend:
Cell 6 computes train_logp = hp.blend(pre["oof_window_logp"], pre["oof_s3"], teacher["public_bagged_oof"]) and test_logp = hp.blend(pre["test_window_logp"], pre["test_s3"], teacher["public_bagged_test"]). The cell 5 markdown calls this "a log-linear blend (0.2 / 0.5 / 0.3)", in the order window model, S3, teacher. The weights are inside hp.blend and do not appear as literals. The resulting OOF macro-F1 is printed but was not saved. The experiment log credits "CatBoost–LightGBM blend with video prototypes" with +0.019 (LB 0.8280) at an earlier stage.
--- graph_and_propagation:
Cell 12 runs hp.apply_white(pre["test_emb"], test_subject, geometry["W"]). Per the cell 11 markdown, embeddings are "centred per participant and whitened (power 0.5)", and the experiment log uses "kNN temperature 0.3". It then sets dd = dict(logp=test_logp, emb=white_test, grp=test_subject, sbj=test_subject, sets={}).

Pass 1: P0 = hp.bag_graph(dd, test_links). This builds a per-participant kNN graph over the 768-d whitened video embeddings, "joined with the timeline links", and averages "five fixed configurations" across all eight timelines. The value of k and the propagation algorithm or parameters are not visible; they live in hp.bag_graph.

Pass 2: P1 = hp.bag_graph(dict(dd, targets=hp.count_targets(k1, counts_1, test_subject)), test_links) propagates towards the learned counts.

Final step: Q_test = hp.calibrated_to_counts(P1, test_subject, hp.count_targets(k2, counts_2, test_subject)).

The DEV graph is NOT recomputed. graph["P0"], graph["P1"] and graph["probabilities"] are loaded from whitened_graph.npz.

Rejected variants: graph smoothing 1.5 with whitening (dev 0.93217, 3/5 folds), analytic covariance shrinkage (0.93177, 1/5), fixed 50/50 original+whitened geometry (LB 0.9265), and a fixed semantic-supernode restart (0.93179, 2/5).
--- links:
The links are precomputed and loaded from strict_rank_margin_timeline_links.npz (dev) and rank_margin_test_links.npz (test). The cell 7 markdown describes the method. For each window, a LambdaRank model scores candidate successors "within that window's candidate set". A one-to-one assignment then turns the scores into chains. One assignment uses the clean scores and seven use fixed perturbations, giving 8 timeline hypotheses. Link confidence is the chosen score minus the best alternative, normalised (score_qn). The experiment log adds "self-supervised successor links" and a "Confidence-adaptive blend of link models".

Link features are not shown. Cell 8 plots per-hypothesis accuracy as (succ[k]==true_succ)[true_succ>=0].mean() and prints the share of windows whose successor is identical in all 8 hypotheses. Neither value was saved.

From the experiment log: "Eight ranked timelines with clean-margin confidence" gave LB 0.9272 (+0.004). One timeline instead of the blend gave 0.9228 (-0.0009). Sixteen timelines gave 0.9259 (-0.0013).
--- counts_constraint:
Cell 12 fits count_model_1 = hp.fit_count_model(graph["P0"], train_logp, subjects, y) and count_model_2 = hp.fit_count_model(graph["P1"], train_logp, subjects, y). The cell 11 markdown says "a ridge model that predicts how many windows each exercise occupies for that participant". Both models are trained on the precomputed, cross-fitted dev graph outputs.

At test time, hp.predict_counts(count_model, P, test_logp, test_subject) returns (k, counts). hp.count_targets(k, counts, test_subject) converts these into per-subject targets, which serve as propagation targets in pass 2 and for the final hp.calibrated_to_counts rescale. There are two passes.

The model uses profile shape only: "Exercise identity in the count model" cost 0.0016 (LB 0.9221). An earlier fixed approach, "VideoMAE similarity graph with a 90 s activity quota", scored 0.7775. "Length-aware activity minimum" fell to 0.7322.
--- refiner:
Cell 15 builds the rows. Dev: keys, X = hp.boundary_rows(graph["probabilities"], pre["oof_window_logp"], pre["oof_s3"], pre["oof_emb"], links, subjects). Test: test_keys, X_test = hp.boundary_rows(Q_test, pre["test_window_logp"], pre["test_s3"], pre["test_emb"], test_links, test_subject). The embeddings passed here are the UNWHITENED oof_emb and test_emb. keys is (n_proposals, 2), holding window index and alternative label. The target is (y[keys[:,0]] == keys[:,1]).astype(int8).

Per the cell 14 markdown, every label change along every hypothesis proposes flipping windows "within a few steps" to the neighbouring label. The radius is not visible. Each proposal gets 89 label-free features averaged over the hypotheses that propose it: evidence margins, neighbour agreement, link confidence and timeline agreement.

Cell 16 trains one model per seed s in hp.REFINER_SEEDS (4 seeds): lgb.train(dict(hp.REFINER_PARAMS, seed=s), lgb.Dataset(X, label=target), num_boost_round=300). It averages their predict(X_test) outputs. Then labels = hp.decode(Q_test.argmax(1), test_keys, scores), which flips a window when the mean score is above 0.5 (markdown; the threshold is inside hp.decode). Cell 17 plots a histogram of the scores with the 0.5 line.

From the experiment log: the boundary consensus refiner added +0.0008 (0.9280) and four seeds added +0.0007 (0.9286). Rejected variants: participant-balanced loss 0.93187 (2/5), refiner prediction before context aggregation 0.93158 (1/5), and single-flip macro-F1 regret 0.93129 (0/5).

--- plug_in_point:
ROW ORDER. All test-side arrays share one order: pre["test_*"], teacher["public_bagged_test"], test_links and test_subject are all indexed in the order of ids = pre["test_id"]. The cell 19 assert guarantees this equals the row order of sample_submission.csv. To align external probabilities, map them by id rather than assuming the order. Load ext (12234,19) with ext_ids, then: pos = {v:i for i,v in enumerate(ext_ids)}; ext = ext[[pos[i] for i in ids.tolist()]].

OPTION A: test-only. No OOF is needed, and this is the cheapest option. In cell 6, right after test_logp = hp.blend(...), insert:
  lp_ext = np.log(np.clip(ext,1e-6,1))
  test_logp = (1-w)*test_logp + w*lp_ext
Then re-normalise to log-softmax with test_logp -= np.logaddexp.reduce(test_logp,1,keepdims=True), but only if np.logaddexp.reduce(hp.blend output,1) is about 0. Check this first, because the graph code may depend on the scale.

Everything downstream on test then picks up the change automatically. In cell 12, dd['logp'] is used by bag_graph passes 1 and 2, and hp.predict_counts(..., test_logp, ...) uses it too. Q_test changes, so the test refiner features and the decode base in cells 15 and 16 change as well. The train side stays as it is (train_logp, graph["P0"], graph["P1"], graph["probabilities"], the refiner training set). That leaves a small train/test distribution shift in two places: the logp input of the count model and the Q_test refiner features. Use a small w (0.1 to 0.3) and treat each value as one LB probe.

OPTION B: fully consistent. You need OOF on exactly the 69,326 dev rows, in the row order of strict_oof_pregraph.npz: shape (69326,19) log-probs, aligned with pre["subject"] and pre["y"], and ideally cross-fitted on pre["fold"], the 5 participant folds. Mapping our stride-25 OOF (n_windows, 4 limbs, 19) onto these rows needs each dev row's recording, start sample and limb. This notebook never loads those; inspect pre.files or read hasca_pipeline.py.

Even with the OOF, blending it into train_logp in cell 6 only reaches hp.fit_count_model. graph["P0"] and graph["P1"], the features of the count model, and graph["probabilities"], the dev input of the refiner, are PRECOMPUTED from the old evidence. Rebuilding them needs the dev-side cross-fitted graph code from hasca_pipeline.py, which this notebook does not call. That code must exclude the outer fold and each profile's own fold in both count passes (cell 20).

The refiner's own window features are pre["oof_window_logp"]/pre["oof_s3"] (dev) and pre["test_window_logp"]/pre["test_s3"] (test). These enter hp.boundary_rows directly, not through the blend. To use external probabilities in the refiner as well, you must replace or add the dev and test pair together, on the same dev rows, so dev OOF is required. Replacing pre["test_s3"] alone would break train/test feature consistency.

Our OOF uses scene-cluster folds rather than participant folds. If the same subject appears in training, OOF confidence may be inflated relative to unseen test subjects, which would bias the count model and the refiner.

--- lineage:
Cell 0 credits:
- CNN and graph ideas: Briano (woominyo), "lb-0-890-wear-hasca-2026-timeline-graph". Cell 3's window_infer.py header says "Derived from Briano (woominyo), WEAR Timeline + Graph, version 7 ... Model architecture, normalization and numerical operations are retained."
- One-to-one assignment: Hanh Tran (honghanhhh), "wear-hasca-hungarian-chain-viterbi-lb-0-74".

Same author's earlier public packaging, jiweiliu/public-fast-gpu-inference (siblings report LB 0.9077), shares:
- the dataset jiweiliu/wear-timeline-graph-models;
- the 0.903 warning text;
- the per-class count constant 97 (parent CFG "per_ex": 97, line 115). The parent used "imu_w": 0.3; this notebook uses 0.2.

New in this variant: four further private datasets. Cell 0 describes them as:
- a "locally fitted 97.5% video-dropout CNN" (soft975_s0/s1);
- a "locally fitted context expert" (LightGBM s3_model.txt plus s3_soft975_model.txt);
- a class-aware full-probability successor model;
- a context-only null gate.
--- compute:
GPU is mandatory. Cell 1 runs `assert GPU_OK and N_GPU >= 2, 'This public kernel requires T4 x2'`, so the CPU-fallback warning is dead code in practice.

- **Window inference (cell 5):** two subprocesses run window_infer.py, one per GPU (CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES 0 and 1). Run names are split alternately, model_names[::2] and model_names[1::2]. Batch WEAR_INFER_BATCH=2048. AMP is fp16 on T4 unless MODEL_ROOT/source_precision.txt == 'cpu_fp32' (then WEAR_AMP=0).
- **CPU work in parallel:** a single ThreadPoolExecutor worker computes hand_features while the GPUs infer.
- **Rest of the notebook:** uses torch cuda for the per-subject similarity matrices in cells 7-10 (N_subject x N_subject, about 3k x 3k).
- **Threads:** OMP/OPENBLAS/MKL threads = 4. LightGBM predict uses num_threads=4 in cell 9.
- **Scratch:** ROOT=/tmp/wear when /tmp has more than 12e9 bytes free (comment: "about 6.5 GB of scratch"), otherwise WORK/_wear. Cell 12 deletes it when WEAR_CLEANUP=1 (the default on Kaggle).
- **Memory, estimated from code shapes, not logged:** test video float16 (12234,15,768) is about 282 MB. Cell 9 builds up to 170 candidates per row and a 74-column float32 pair matrix, so expect hundreds of MB.
- **Unused settings:** WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN=150 is defined but never used. SMOKE/SMOKE_SUBJECTS are unused.
- **Runtime:** no runtime logs exist. Stage times are printed by the `stage` context manager and in cell 12, but there are no outputs.

Reads {run}_model_f{0..4}.pt for every run name matching '*_model_f0.pt' (must include final* and imu* runs) and source_precision.txt ('cpu_fp32' or 'gpu_amp').",

"GATE_ROOT = parent of 'context_null_gate.txt' (likely jiweiliu/wear-context-nullgate-private-models). LightGBM null gate.",
"SOFT_ROOT = parent of 's3_soft975_model.txt' (jiweiliu/wear-soft975-context-private-models). Reads s3_soft975_model.txt (LightGBM soft context expert) and soft975_s{0,1}_model_f{0..4}.pt (97.5% video-dropout CNNs).",
"Each root is asserted unique with len(hits)==1, so attaching a second copy of any of these files breaks cell 1. The dataset-to-root mapping is inferred from file names; the notebook does not state it."
]
--- tile_construction:
Not applicable. The notebook never builds train tiles and never reads train data, labels or label corrections. It has no sbj_10, sbj_2 or sbj_7 handling and no window offsets, stride, limb sampling or label rule; all of that happened in undisclosed local fitting.

The only data structure built is on test (cell 2):
- Rows are kept in test_meta_data.csv row order and saved as PROC/test_id.npy.
- One limb per row: sensor index from SENSORS = ["right_arm","right_leg","left_leg","left_arm"] mapped to 0..3, saved to test_sensor.npy (int8).
- Video is transposed to (N,15,768) float16.

Window-CNN normalisation (cell 3) is transductive and per test subject:
- Accelerometer mean/std per (test subject, sensor) over that subject's own test samples; requires more than 100 samples, else 0/1.
- Video mean/std per test subject over all frames.
- Test groups are offset by SB = list(range(22)), commented "source training has 22 subjects".
--- cv_protocol:
The notebook contains no CV. What exists is in the markdown and comments:
- Every window-CNN run has 5 fold checkpoints (_model_f0.._f4), averaged at test time.
- Cell 0: k=5 graph and 0.75 link weight "selected on clean subject-held-out local validation".
- Cell 0: gate family weights 60/0/30% are "locally validated"; cell 11 comment: "median of held-subject selections on the local OOF: jogging, stretching, exercise".
- Cell 0: 0.85 threshold and 25% neighbor weight "checked on local subject-held-out OOF".
- Cell 0: 75% context weight "passed held-subject development and prospective transfer validation".
- Cell 11 comment: "A single light pass improves all five held-subject OOF folds".
- Cell 9 comment: the class-aware mutual-best rule is "the same score and selection rule as the held-subject OOF check".

So the folds were five subject-held-out folds, but fold membership, seeds and OOF numbers are not given. No seeds are set anywhere in the notebook (inference only).
--- window_models:
[
{
"name": "final_* fusion CNN runs (at least final_s0; full list printed only at runtime)",

"details": "Cell 3 class Net(d=256):\n- Sensor embedding: nn.Embedding(4,32).\n- IMU branch: 5 ConvBlocks (Conv1d + BN + GELU + residual) with widths and kernels (4+32->64,k7), (64->128,k5), (128->128,k5,d2), (128->256,k3,d4), (256->256,k3,d8), then mean+max pool -> Linear(512,256).\n- IMU input: subject-normalised xyz plus (|a|-1).\n- Video 'pool' arch: Dropout(vdrop) -> Linear(768,256) -> GELU -> Dropout(.2), then mean+std over 15 frames -> Linear(512,256).\n- Fuse head: Linear(512+32,256) -> GELU -> Dropout(.3) -> Linear(256,19). Auxiliary h_imu/h_vid heads exist but only logits[0] (fusion) is used.\n- Weights: MODEL_ROOT/{run}_model_f{0..4}.pt. --- window_blend:
Cell 6, "W4 fixed-CV window blend":
- normalized_run(name) re-applies log-softmax to each run's test_logp_raw.npy.
- fus = mean of normalized final* runs (log space); imu = mean of normalized imu* runs. model_names here come from MODEL_ROOT only, so soft975 is excluded.
- logits = fus + .2 * imu, then softmax gives prob. window_prob = prob.copy().
- soft_prob is kept separate: the mean of exp(normalized_run) over soft975_s0 and soft975_s1.

The second-level combination in cell 8 is .25 log(parent S3) + .75 log(soft S3), renormalised.

No OOF is reported for any of these. The IMU weight .2 differs from the parent's imu_w 0.3.
--- graph_and_propagation:
Embedding: emb = final_s0 test_emb, the window mean of per-subject-normalised VideoMAE features, (N,768). There is no PC removal or whitening.

S3 kNN features (cell 8): per subject, cosine top-10 neighbors of emb with the diagonal excluded.

G1 (cell 10), propagate_links(link_src, link_dst, k_neighbors=10, beta=.5), run per subject:
1. Link adjacency: wl is the symmetric 0/1 adjacency of the link set; al is wl row-normalised; ld is the link degree.
2. Embedding smoothing: e = l2norm(emb); e = l2norm(e + .5*(al@e)).
3. Affinity: sim = e@e.T + .5*(sqrt(p)@sqrt(p).T), where p = initial = S3 prob, with the diagonal set to -inf.
4. Graph: keep top-k; weights exp((val - val.max())/.1); symmetrise with w.maximum(w.T); av is w row-normalised.
5. Link mixing: link_beta = beta*min(ld/2,1) per row.
6. Propagation, 3 rounds:
   - Round 0: f0 = initial.
   - Rounds 1-2: q = normalize(f)**1.5, then 50 Sinkhorn iterations to the count target; f0 = normalize(sqrt(initial*q)).
   - Each round runs 20 iterations of f = .93*((1-link_beta)*(av@f) + link_beta*(al@f)) + .07*f0.

Three graphs:
- cur_mutual = propagate_links(L1 mutual video links), k=10, beta .5.
- cur_hungarian = propagate_links(L2 Hungarian links from successor_model), k=10, beta .5.
- cur_full_union = propagate_links(class-aware Hungarian ∪ L2 mutual links), k_neighbors=5, beta=.75.

Final blend (cell 11): baseline = .25*final_calibration(cur_mutual) + .75*final_calibration(cur_hungarian); calibrated = .5*baseline + .5*final_calibration(cur_full_union).
--- links:
**L1, mutual video-boundary links (cell 7, per subject, GPU).**
- Frames are L2-normalised. head = normalised mean of the first 3 frames; tail = normalised mean of the last 3.
- sim = tail@head.T + .5*(lastframe@firstframe.T), with the diagonal set to -inf.
- Kept only where the row argmax is mutual (predecessor argmax over columns points back).
- Results: successor[src]=dst, default -1; link_score = sim, default -50. Used in the S3 neighbor_block and in graph cur_mutual.

**L2 candidates and features (cell 9).**
- Per subject, the union of top-100 by th = tail@head, top-40 by lf = last@first, and top-30 by mm = mean@mean, deduplicated.
- 17 pair features:
  - 6 video features: th[r,c], lf[r,c], mm[r,c], th[c,r], th[r,c] - rowmax(th), th[r,c] - colmax(th).
  - 11 features from window_prob (the cell 6 blend, not the S3 prob): sum(a*b), sum(sqrt(a*b)), L1 |a-b|, same argmax, a.max, b.max, a[0], b[0], same sensor, and only when the sensors match, log1p of the accelerometer jump ||last_acc[src] - first_acc[dst]|| and log1p of the extrapolated jump ||2*last - prev - first||.

**L2 scoring and selection.**
- CONTEXT_ROOT/successor_model.txt gives scores.
- Mutual best out/in gives mutual_src/mutual_dst.
- Per-subject one-to-one assignment: scipy min_weight_full_bipartite_matching on a sparse cost of 20 - logit(clip(score,1e-6,1-1e-6)), with a self-loop (no-successor) cost of 40.0 on the diagonal. Pairs with row != col are kept and stored as undirected unique edges (src,dst), the 'hungarian_links'.

**Class-aware scorer.**
- FULLPROB_ROOT/fullprob_successor_model.txt on 74 features: 17 pair features + window_prob[src] (19) + window_prob[dst] (19) + |diff| (19).
- The same Hungarian procedure, unioned with mutual_src/dst from the first scorer, gives full_src/full_dst ('classaware_union_links').
- High-confidence links: mutual-best pairs under full_scores with score >= .85 give high_src/high_dst/high_score.

No link accuracy is reported or logged.
--- counts_constraint:
A soft per-subject class-marginal constraint via Sinkhorn, with no hard assignment. Per subject with n windows:
- null = max(n - 18*97, .05*n);
- target[0] = null; target[1..18] = (n - null)/18 each.

So each of the 18 activities is assumed to last about 97 one-second windows per subject (constant 97 matches the parent's "per_ex": 97).

Applied:
1. Inside propagate_links rounds 1-2 (cell 10) with q = normalize(f)**1.5, then 50 iterations of column scaling to target followed by row renormalisation.
2. In final_calibration (cell 11) on each of the three graph outputs, with the same power 1.5, 50 iterations and target.

No other each-workout-once constraint exists on classes. The one-to-one constraint applies only to successor links (bipartite matching, cell 9).
--- refiner:
There is no boundary refiner. The final stages are in cell 11:
1. **Null gate by family.** family = np.digitize(argmax(calibrated[:,1:]) + 1, [6, 11]): 0 = jogging classes 1-5, 1 = stretching 6-10, 2 = exercise 11-18. gate_weight = [.6, 0., .3][family]. calibrated[:,0] = (1-gw)*calibrated[:,0] + gw*gate_p_null. The non-null columns are rescaled to (1 - p0) * activity_ratio, preserving their original ratios.
2. **High-confidence successor smoothing.** high_w is a symmetric sparse matrix with weight high_score on high_src<->high_dst. neighbor = (high_w @ calibrated)/degree, falling back to the row itself when degree is 0. calibrated = .75*calibrated + .25*neighbor.
3. **Output.** prediction = calibrated.argmax(1). submission.csv has columns id (from PROC/test_id.npy, meta row order) and target_feature.

--- plug_in_point:
**What needs aligning.** Every array in the notebook is in test_meta_data.csv row order, saved as PROC/test_id.npy. The submission writes ids from the same file.
- Load our (12234,19) test probabilities plus our id vector from an attached dataset, then reorder by id: `ids = np.load(PROC/'test_id.npy'); pos = pd.Series(np.arange(len(ext_ids)), index=ext_ids); P_ext = P_ext[pos.loc[ids].to_numpy()]`.
- Columns must follow CLASSES order in cell 2: 0 null, 1 jogging, ..., 18 bench-dips.
- Clip to 1e-9 and renormalise rows.
- Add our dataset to kernel-metadata dataset_sources. Avoid shipping files named link_model.txt, s3_model.txt, etc.; cell 1 asserts exactly one hit for each.

**Train-tile OOF is not needed by this notebook.** Every downstream learner is a frozen, locally trained LightGBM: s3_model, s3_soft975_model, context_null_gate, successor_model, fullprob_successor_model. All blend weights are hard-coded and nothing is refit in-notebook. Our stride-25 4-limb OOF therefore has no slot here, and the external weight must be chosen blind on the LB or tuned elsewhere (a pipeline that retrains with OOF).

There are three insertion options, safest first.

**Option B, recommended: no distribution shift for any booster.**
- Where: cell 8, right after `prob /= prob.sum(1, keepdims=True)` (line 739), before the gate model.
- Code: `prob = np.exp((1-W_EXT)*np.log(np.clip(prob,1e-9,1)) + W_EXT*np.log(np.clip(P_ext,1e-9,1))); prob /= prob.sum(1, keepdims=True)`.
- This prob becomes `raw` in G1 (cell 10). There it is both the propagation prior `initial` and the sqrt-prob affinity term in sim, then flows through count calibration and argmax.
- The gate, links and S3 features are unaffected because they were computed from x_s3 and window_prob before this point.

**Option A: window-level, feeds everything downstream, with feature shift.**
- Where: cell 6, between `prob /= prob.sum(1, keepdims=True)` (line 662) and `window_prob = prob.copy()` (line 663).
- Apply the same geometric blend.
- This changes:
  - lp, which is x_s3 columns 361:380 and also feeds the null gate;
  - neighbor_block context, x_s3 cols 418:512;
  - the nn neighbor means, cols 512:533;
  - all 11 derived successor pair features and the 57 class-aware features through window_prob.
- fus (cols 380:399) and imu (399:418) stay CNN-only.
- Every booster was trained on the author's CNN OOF distribution, so keep W_EXT small (~0.2-0.3) or apply it to window_prob only for links.

**Option C: an extra graph.**
- Where: cell 10, after the three propagate_links calls and before `del raw, prob, emb`.
- Code: `raw = P_ext; cur_ext = propagate_links(full_src, full_dst, k_neighbors=5, beta=.75)`. raw is read by propagate_links as a closure global, so it must be reassigned first.
- Then in cell 11: `calibrated = (1-w)*(.5*baseline + .5*final_calibration(cur_full_union)) + w*final_calibration(cur_ext)`, before the null-gate block.
- Optionally also blend our p(null) into calibrated[:,0] the same way gate_p_null is blended.

**For cheap iteration.** Insert np.save calls into WORK so the arrays survive cleanup:
- in cell 6: window_prob, fus, imu, soft_prob, emb;
- in cell 8: prob after S3, gate_p_null;
- in cell 9: mutual_src/dst, src/dst, full_src/full_dst, high_src/high_dst/high_score;
- in cell 11: calibrated.

Then G1 and C1 (torch-cuda topk only) can be re-run locally on CPU with a different W_EXT without rerunning the GPU stages.

NOTEBOOK: jiweiliu/public-fast-gpu-inference ("public fast GPU inference"). Source: /Users/randy/Desktop/Kaggle/wear/ref2/public-fast-gpu-inference/public-fast-gpu-inference.txt (16 cells, 0-15). Metadata: enab
LB: The title and markdown state no LB. The no-GPU warning in cell 1 says "the score will be far below 0.903 ... Select the GPU T4 x2 accelerator to reproduce the LB score", which implies about 0.903 public LB. The lineage notebook (Briano lb-0-890..., ref2/lb-0-890-wear-hasca-2026-timeline-graph) claim
--- lineage:
Cell 0: derived from Briano (woominyo), "WEAR Timeline + Graph v7" (kaggle.com/code/woominyo/lb-0-890-wear-hasca-2026-timeline-graph), Apache 2.0. Briano credits Hanh Tran for the first link stage. The lineage markdown names this as "WEAR@HASCA Hungarian Chain Viterbi LB 0.74" by honghanhhh: L0 candidate generation, pair features, LGB scorer, Hungarian assignment with cycle breaking. Jiwei Liu's changes: load fitted models, share preprocessing across model passes, use both T4 GPUs (one subprocess per GPU), and "preserves the original prediction formulas". window_infer.py checks the optimised path against the source predict() with assert max_abs < 1e-5 (cell 4). Several training-only functions are copied over unchanged but never called: train_tiles, pair_dataset, true_successor, subject_stats, fit_ridge, Gram, pca, context_baseline, link_smooth, macro_f1, TAB_PARAMS, LK_PARAMS, L0_ROUNDS, LK_NEG, LK_ROUNDS, TRAIN_SETS.
--- compute:
Requires GPU T4 x2. Cell 1 runs `assert GPU_OK and N_GPU >= 2, 'This public kernel requires T4 x2'`, after a subprocess CUDA probe that runs torch.ones(8,'cuda').sum(). The GPU is used only in cell 5:
- Two subprocesses run `python window_infer.py --data PROC --out RUNS_DIR/gpu_{g} --vnorm subject --anorm subject --vid_arch pool`, each with CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=g, OMP_NUM_THREADS=1, WEAR_INFER_BATCH=2048.
- WEAR_AMP is "0" if MODEL_ROOT/source_precision.txt == "cpu_fp32", else "1" (fp16 autocast on T4, capability 7.5).
- GPU 0 gets sorted(model_names)[::2] and GPU 1 gets [1::2]. The script comment says each process handles 3 runs.

Everything else is CPU numpy/scipy/LightGBM with BLAS threads set to 4 ("Kaggle has 4 vCPUs"). The heaviest CPU work is cell 12/13 subject_all, which builds several (n,n) float32 similarity matrices per test subject.

Scratch ROOT is /tmp/wear if /tmp has more than 12e9 bytes free, else WORK/_wear; the comment says "about 6.5 GB of scratch", inherited from the training version. No runtime is logged. The stage() context manager prints wall time and RSS per stage, but there are no outputs. WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN=150 is defined but unused here. The lineage markdown gives about 30 min for its CPU stages on Kaggle's 4 vCPUs (training version).

--- tile_construction:
This notebook builds no train tiles at runtime, because it is inference only. The code that is present describes how the loaded models were trained.

(1) train_tiles(rec_ids, seed), cell 7. Unused here. It reads PROC/recordings.json, which this notebook never creates, so calling it would fail. Recipe:
- Starts s = np.arange(0, len(acc)-50+1, 50), kept if s*3//5 + 8 + 15 <= len(vid).
- Valid-limb mask okw = no missing sample in [s, s+50), via a cumsum of ~valid.
- rng = np.random.default_rng(seed + i*17), where i is the recording index.
- Limb drawn uniformly among valid limbs by inverse CDF: cp = cumsum(okw/okw.sum), limbs = min((rng.random(n)[:,None] > cp).sum(1), 3).
- IMU = acc[s + arange(50), limb] in SENSORS order ["right_arm","right_leg","left_leg","left_arm"].
- Video frames = (s*3//5) + 8 + arange(15).
- No labels. The link target is true_successor: the tile with start+50 in the same recording; pair_dataset marks cand == row+1 as positive.

(2) subject_stats (cell 11, unused) builds the ridge training pairs: starts at STRIDE_B=10 samples, the successor's video frames are +30 frames (= +50 samples), and both i and j must be fully valid.

(3) The lineage notebook's OOF tile set, which trained the S3 stacker, the QN_REF table and the graph-config ranking:
- oof_windows(): STRIDE=5 windows, then only start % 50 == 0 is kept.
- Label = majority (argmax of label counts over the 50 samples).
- Windows where no limb is fully valid are dropped.
- Limb chosen per fold with rng = np.random.default_rng(1234 + fold), by the same inverse-CDF rule.
- 69,326 rows; fold sizes [14353, 12995, 14625, 12768, 14585]; sha1 checks: sensor f81d90085315, (rec,start) e4177f2589d7.
- Recording index = sorted(INPUT/train/inertial_feat/*.csv) order.

Neither file has any label corrections or exclusions: no sbj_10 last session, sbj_2 last 112 s, or sbj_7 delay handling. A grep for these terms found nothing. Test tiles are taken as given; test sensor ids come from meta[loc_col] mapped through SENSORS.
--- cv_protocol:
This notebook has no CV, because everything is pre-fitted. The test window probabilities are the mean of 5 fold models per run: np.mean over folds of log_softmax, renormalised later in load_runs.

The pre-fitted models come from the lineage protocol:
- 5 subject folds (FOLDS=5), greedily balanced by window count: subjects in descending size order are assigned to the least-loaded fold.
- Window NN seeds: torch.manual_seed(seed*100 + fold), with seed 0..3 for final runs and 0..1 for imu runs.
- L0 OOF links come from per-fold scorers; the test L0 scorer is trained on all recordings.
- Ridges are fit per fold, and test averages all 5.
- The combined link scorer was trained once, for test only.
- Tabular experts were trained on all 69,326 OOF rows with fixed rounds (S3 60, T 113) "from nested early stopping".
- The comment above TEST_CFGS says the graph configs are the "test top-5 (ranked on all 5 folds with quantile-normalised combined links)".
- The QN_REF table is the "1001-point quantile table of our pooled linked OOF combined-link scores ('all', 22 subjects)".
--- window_models:

"details": "Net(d=256) in window_infer.py (cell 4).\n- IMU input: (a - AM[g,sensor])/AS[g,sensor], with stats per test subject and limb from that subject's own windows (acc_stats, needs more than 100 samples), concatenated with |a|-1 to give 4 channels, plus a 32-d sensor embedding broadcast over time.\n- IMU branch: ConvBlocks (36->64 k7, 64->128 k5, 128->128 k5 d2, 128->256 k3 d4, 256->256 k3 d8; Conv+BN+GELU plus a 1x1 skip), then mean and max pooling, then Linear(512,256)+GELU.\n- Video branch: (v-VM[g])/VS[g] with per-test-subject mean/std over all its frames (+1e-3), then Dropout(0.5)+Linear(768,256)+GELU+Dropout(0.2), mean and std pooling over the 15 frames, then Linear(512,256)+GELU+Dropout(0.3).\n- Fusion head: Linear(512+32,256)+GELU+Dropout(0.3)+Linear(256,19). Only the fusion head logits[0] are used; h_imu and h_vid are ignored.\n- Normalisation groups: G = 22 train placeholders + test subjects (test group = 22 + index); only the test groups are filled.\n- Files: MODEL_ROOT/{run}_model_f{0..4}.pt, written as RUNS_DIR/{run}/test_logp_raw.npy (12234,19), the mean of the 5 folds' log_softmax.\n- Lineage: 12 epochs, 300 steps x batch 512, EMA 0.999, augmentation = rotation of up to 20 deg + scale 0.9-1.1 + noise 0.02.",

"oof_score": "Lineage: window + tabular experts stage OOF 0.877 (cumulative pipeline)"

--- window_blend:
Cell 6, load_runs(runs, 'test'): each run's test_logp_raw is renormalised to a proper log-softmax, then mean_logp averages the runs in probability space: log(mean(exp) + 1e-8). This produces _fus_t over the final* runs and _imu_t over the imu* runs.

blend(fus, imu, wi=CFG['imu_w']=0.3): x = fus + 0.3*imu in log space, then log-softmax. This gives t['logp'], with t['fusion_logp']=_fus_t and t['imu_logp']=_imu_t.

Cell 10 then builds the post-tabular blend:

    TEST_LOGP_B = tab_blend(t['logp'], [(TAB_TEST['S3'],0.5),(TAB_TEST['T'],0.3)])

which is log-softmax(0.2*window + 0.5*S3 + 0.3*T), with TAB_TEST_CHOICE = (("S3",0.5),("T",0.3)).

TEST_LOGP_B is the prior for the final graph stage. OOF (lineage markdown and print strings only, not computed here): fusion x4 0.7194, IMU x2 0.5995, blend 0.7253 (the overview table rounds this to 0.725). Adding the tabular experts reaches 0.877 in the cumulative pipeline.
--- graph_and_propagation:
There are two propagation uses.

(A) PLP_TEST (cell 8), used only as features for the combined-link scorer:
- plp_of = label_prop(exp(t['logp']), t['emb'], sbj, k=10, tau=0.1, use_p=0.5, alpha=0.9, iters=20), from CFG['lp']=(10,0.1,0.5,0.9).
- Per subject: E is the L2-normalised emb.
- Similarity S = E E^T + 0.5*sqrt(P)sqrt(P)^T, with the diagonal set to -inf.
- Keep the top-10 neighbours, weighted W = exp((s - s_max_global)/0.1), symmetrised with W.maximum(W.T), then symmetrically normalised: D^-1/2 W D^-1/2.
- Iterate 20 times: f = 0.9*Wn f + 0.1*f0. Rows are then renormalised.

(B) Final graph, cell 14: graph_P(dt, TEST_CFGS).
- dt = {logp: TEST_LOGP_B, emb: t['emb'], grp/sbj: test sbj, succ: L2_SUCC, score: L2_SCORE_QN, sets: {}}.
- Per-subject video feature: t['emb'] = window mean over the 15 frames of per-test-subject-normalised VideoMAE ((v-VM)/VS). It is written by the GPU job that runs final_s0 (vid_emb) and does not depend on model weights. There is no PC removal.
- Link graph: Lw = link_W(succ, score_qn, b=-2.0, T=1.0, power=1.0), weights sigmoid(score_qn + 2.0), symmetric between succ and pred.
- Embedding smoothing: smooth_emb with g=0.5, h=1: Es = E + 0.5*row_norm(Lw) @ E, where E is unit-normalised.
- kNN: SubjectKNN with K=40 cached, similarity Es Es^T + use_p(0.5)*sqrt(P0)sqrt(P0)^T, sorted. knn_W uses the top k=10 with weights exp((v - vmax)/tau=0.1), symmetrised with max, then row-normalised (norm='row').
- Propagation (label_prop_generic): link_mode 'abs' gives a per-row link weight bb = beta*min(dl/2, 1), where dl = the row sum of Lw. Each of 20 iterations computes g = (1-bb)*A f + bb*row_norm(Lw) f, then f = alpha*g + (1-alpha)*f0. Output rows are renormalised.
- Self-training: rounds-1 extra rounds. Each computes Q = calibrate(norm(F**2.0)) (Sinkhorn with per_ex 97, null_min 0.05), sets the new prior norm(P0**(1-w) * Q**w), and propagates again. The kNN neighbours stay cached from P0 (Qsrc=P0, key 'p0'), and alpha2 = alpha.
- Configs (parse_cfg pattern g{emb_g}/{abs|row}{beta}/b{link_b}/a{alpha}/r{rounds}w{w}), TEST_CFGS:
  - g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r3w0.5
  - g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5
  - g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r2w0.7
  - g0.5/abs0.6/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5
  - g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r2w0.7
- Runner defaults: tau 0.1, k 10, iters 20, use_p 0.5, K=40, T=2.0.
- P_TEST is the row-normalised mean of the 5 configs' F.

link_smooth/context_baseline (CFG link_alpha 0.5, link_iters 10) is defined but unused here.
--- links:
There are two link stages.

L0 (cell 7, l0_model.txt). Used only by S3's neighbor_block, not by the graph.
- pair_features: frames are unit-normalised. Similarity matrices:
  - th = tail3-mean · head3-mean
  - lf = last · first
  - mm = mean · mean
- Candidates: the union of top-k by th (k=_K_TH=40), lf (_K_11=15) and mm (_K_MP=15), deduplicated.
- 12 features: fwd, frame, pooled, rev, dom_r, dom_c, margin, log1p rank_row, log1p rank_col, same-limb, plus log1p gap and log1p ext, which are set only when the limb is the same.
- logodds = log(p/(1-p)) of the LGB prediction; invalid pairs get -50.
- l0_assign: Hungarian (linear_sum_assignment on -cost; default cost -50, diagonal -1e4), drop edges below min_logodds=-1.0, break each cycle at its weakest edge.
- Output: t['succ'] and t['score'], per subject.

L2 "combined links E" (cells 11-13). Used by the graph.
- subject_all() builds candidates from 11 SOURCES (matrix, row/column, k): th r40, lf r15, mm r15, lfc r15, imu r8, imu c5, extlin r25, extlin c10, lfz c30, ext2 r25, dual r25.
  - lfc/lfz/extlin/ext2/thc/dual use subject-centred unit frames; dual is a log-softmax(L/0.05) row+column score; imu = -||(2a_last - a_prev) - a_j,first||, same limb only.
- Unique pairs are counted by n_src and capped at KCAP=160 per row, keeping the pairs most sources agree on.
- Added on top: the ridge-score top KNEW=20 for each ridge model (n_src=0). Ridge score: rs_mat(ll_f, ll_b), from Gaussian forward/backward boundary log-likelihoods of linear predictions of the next window's head-frame block from the current window's [Last, Tail, Head, Mean] blocks, and vice versa.
- Features:
  - BASE49 (OLD30 + NEW_DECODE minus the 4 CA_FEATS), stored as float16. They include bc_w (Bhattacharyya of sqrt(exp(t['logp']))), and bc_lp, maxp_i, maxp_j from PLP_TEST.
  - NEW16 per ridge model: ll_f, ll_b, their dominances, rs and its dominances/ranks, cos_f, cos_b, raw_f, imu_f, imu_b (imu_* from per-limb-pair IMU ridge Gaussians).
- LINK_MODEL.predict(concat(base, new[v])) for each ridge variant v; the mean log-odds over len(RIDGES) variants (the comment says 5).
- lk_assign: Hungarian, drop edges with cost <= floor+1 = -49, break cycles.
- Assert: successors are unique and stay within the same subject.
- qnorm: per subject, the rank of each linked score is mapped to a quantile of QN_REF (the 1001-point OOF table), giving L2_SCORE_QN.

Reported reference numbers (print strings only): "test linked ... (ours 0.951); raw median score ... (ours 0.52)". Lineage L0 OOF: linked 0.924, exact 0.330, same-label 0.887. There is no link accuracy computed in this notebook.
--- counts_constraint:
calibrate(P, sbj, sets, per_ex=97, null_min=0.05, iters=50), cell 2. Per subject:
- null target = max(n - 18*97*sets.get(sbj, 1), 0.05*n).
- Each of the 18 exercise columns targets (n - null)/18.
- 50 Sinkhorn iterations: scale columns to the targets, then renormalise rows.

Test uses sets={}, so every test subject is assumed to have done the circuit once. TRAIN_SETS={0:2, 14:2} is defined but unused here.

It is applied in two places:
- Inside graph self-training (Runner.cal): norm(P**2.0) then calibrate.
- In finish(), cell 2/14: Q = clip(P,1e-12)**(1/sharpen_T=0.5), i.e. P squared, row-normalised, calibrate, then argmax.

The printed reference null shares per test subject are "(ours: 22 0.66, 23 0.43, 24 0.15, 25 0.09)".
--- refiner:
None. There is no boundary refiner and no further final stage. The final step (cell 14) is:
1. P_TEST = graph_P(dt, TEST_CFGS).
2. labels = finish(P_TEST, dt): sharpen by squaring, Sinkhorn count calibration, argmax.
3. Merge into sample_submission on its id column.
4. Write integer class indices 0-18 (CLASSES order) to WORK/submission.csv.

--- plug_in_point:
Row order and class order:
- Every test array here is in test_meta_data.csv row order: PROC/test_id.npy = meta['id'], and t['sbj'] and t['sensor'] are aligned with it.
- Reorder external probabilities by id, not by assumption: ids = np.load(PROC/'test_id.npy', allow_pickle=True); P_ext = ext_df.set_index('id').loc[ids, cols].to_numpy(), or index a numpy array via a dict id->row.
- Class columns must follow CLASSES in cell 3 (0 null, 1 jogging, 2 jogging (rotating arms), ..., 18 bench-dips).
- Convert to normalised log-probs: EXT = np.log(np.clip(P_ext,1e-7,1)); EXT -= logsumexp(EXT, 1, keepdims=True).

OPTION A, recommended and lowest risk. Insert at the end of cell 10, after `TEST_LOGP_B = tab_blend(...)`. Replace that line with an explicit blend:

    x = (1-w_e)*TEST_LOGP_B + w_e*EXT

then log-softmax. An alternative is `tab_blend(t['logp'], [(TAB_TEST['S3'],0.5),(TAB_TEST['T'],0.3),(EXT,w_e)])`, but that silently reduces the window weight to 0.2 - w_e and goes negative if w_e > 0.2. TEST_LOGP_B feeds only cell 14: dt['logp'] -> P0 of label propagation, the use_p prediction-similarity term of the kNN, and the self-training priors. The pretrained S3 and link scorer keep receiving the source-NN inputs they were trained on.

OPTION B, deeper. In cell 6, replace or blend t['logp'] (for example a log-space or probability-space mix of blend(_fus_t,_imu_t,0.3) and EXT). Optionally also set t['fusion_logp'] and t['imu_logp']. This propagates into:
- PLP_TEST (cell 8), which drives the link features bc_lp, maxp_i, maxp_j;
- bc_w in subject_all (cell 13 passes t['logp'][ii] and PLP_TEST[ii]);
- the S3 input features (logp, fusion_logp and imu_logp columns, neighbor_block logp/ctx, knn_block);
- the 0.2 window term of TEST_LOGP_B.

tab_S3.txt and link_model.txt were trained on the source NN's OOF distribution (window OOF about 0.725), so feeding sharper or differently calibrated probabilities is a covariate shift. qnorm is rank-based, so the link-score scale stays protected.

OOF on train tiles is NOT required to run this notebook. Nothing here uses OOF; all models are pre-fitted. OOF would be needed only to tune w_e or to retrain S3, the link scorer, QN_REF or the graph configs. That has to be done in the lineage notebook (ref2/lb-0-890-wear-hasca-2026-timeline-graph), whose OOF rows are its sim_meta: 69,326 tiles with fields (rec, start, sensor, y, sbj, fold).
- rec = index in sorted(train/inertial_feat/*.csv).
- start % 50 == 0.
- sensor drawn with default_rng(1234+fold).
- Mapping our (n_windows,4,19) stride-25 OOF: row = recording offset + start//25 (if our index starts at 0 with stride 25 and the same end rule), limb = sim['sensor'] remapped to SENSORS order.
- Our scene-cluster folds differ from the lineage's 5 subject folds. That is acceptable for stacking only if every row's prediction comes from a model that never saw that subject.

If the window NNs are dropped entirely, t['emb'] must still be provided, since the graph and PLP need it. Recompute it as the per-test-subject (v-mean)/(std+1e-3) frame normalisation, averaged over the 15 frames (vid_emb in window_infer.py). Without that, the GPU stage must still run final_s0, which writes test_emb.npy.

To export intermediates for offline blending, set WEAR_CLEANUP=0 or np.save to /kaggle/working before cell 15: TEST_LOGP_B, P_TEST, L2_SUCC, L2_SCORE_QN, PLP_TEST, t['logp']. These are small, about 0.9 MB per (12234,19) float32.
--- tunable_knobs:
[
"CFG['imu_w']=0.3: fusion vs IMU-only log-space blend (cell 2, used in cell 6)",
"TAB_TEST_CHOICE=(('S3',0.5),('T',0.3)), giving window weight 0.2 (cell 10); TAB_ROUNDS {'S3':60,'T':113} (cell 10)",

"Per-limb test IMU stats need more than 100 samples (acc_stats len(xs) > 100); otherwise they default to mean 0, std 1 for that (subject, limb)",

--- lineage:
The L0 timeline-link code (candidate generation, 12 pair features, LightGBM link scorer, Hungarian assignment with cycle breaking) is adapted from the public notebook "WEAR@HASCA Hungarian Chain Viterbi LB 0.74" by honghanhhh (Apache 2.0), per cell 0 and cell 15. The combined-link (L2) features extend that code. The window models, tabular experts (T, S3), graph propagation and Sinkhorn calibration are the author's own, copied from their chain.py, postproc.py, final_decode.py and exp_common.py (cell 3). The notebook is released under Apache 2.0. The kernel owner slug is woominyo; the task note calls the author "Briano".
--- compute:
Compute figures come from markdown only; no logged runtime exists. Cell 1 says a GPU is needed for the window models (T4 x2: about 40 min; one T4: about 65 min). CPU stages take about 30 min on Kaggle's 4 vCPUs. The whole notebook takes about 1.5-2 h. WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN defaults to 150 min (env WEAR_WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN); a time guard skips imu_s1, then final_s3 and final_s2. The CPU fallback window stage takes about 1.5 h.

Each training subprocess loads all train acc and video onto the GPU ("stagger the 3.2 GB data loads"). Mixed precision is fp16+GradScaler on T4, bf16 on Ampere and fp32 on P100/CPU. A run that fails is retried once in fp32.

Scratch: ROOT=/tmp/wear if /tmp has more than 12 GB free, else WORK/_wear. That is "about 6.5 GB of scratch". The ridge Gram (3841x3841 float64 per subject) is computed on the GPU when one is available ("saves about 5 min"). OMP/OPENBLAS/MKL threads are set to 4, LightGBM num_threads=4.

Many per-subject (n,n) float32/int32 matrices are built (L0 pair_features rank_row/rank_col, L2 subject_all mats, ll_mats, ranks_at, sorted float64 copies). These dominate RAM; subjects 0 and 14 recorded twice, so they are the largest. The stage class prints RSS and peak, but no values are logged.

kernel-metadata: enable_gpu=false, enable_internet=false, only competition_sources.

--- tile_construction:
PREP (cell 6, prep()):
- Recordings are sorted(INPUT/train/inertial_feat/*.csv); that list index is "rec", saved in PROC/recordings.json.
- acc (T,12) float32 in g. NaN values are linearly interpolated (pandas interpolate, limit_direction='both') then fillna(0).
- valid (T,4) = no NaN per limb.
- lab: label strings are mapped to CLASSES indices 0..18 (0=null); NaN becomes 'null'.
- Asserts: 24 recordings, sum T=3,466,400, sum F=2,079,840, F*5==T*3.
- Limb order SENSORS=["right_arm","right_leg","left_leg","left_arm"] gives indices 0..3.

OOF TILES (cell 8, oof_windows()):
- For each recording: starts = np.arange(0, T-50+1, STRIDE=5), kept only if start*3//5 + 8 + 15 <= F.
- Label y = argmax of per-class sample counts in [start, start+50), i.e. majority. Ties go to the lowest class id.
- W_ok (n,4) = limb has zero invalid samples in the window. Windows with no valid limb are dropped.
- Folds: subjects sorted by descending stride-5 window count, each assigned greedily to the least-loaded of FOLDS=5.
- Per fold: keep only start % 50 == 0, i.e. non-overlapping 1-s tiles aligned to sample 0. rng = np.random.default_rng(1234 + fold). cp = cumsum(W_ok/W_ok.sum(1)); sensor = (rng.random(n)[:,None] > cp).sum(1), a uniform draw among valid limbs.
- Row order: fold 0..4, then rec index, then start ascending.
- load_inputs: IMU = acc[start:start+50, sensor] (N,50,3) float32. Video frames v[(start*3//5) + 8 + arange(15)], i.e. frame offsets 8..22 of floor(0.6*start) (N,15,768) float16.
- Saved as ROOT/sim_acc.npy, ROOT/sim_vid.npy and ROOT/sim_meta.npz (rec, start, sensor, y, sbj, fold).
- Asserts: 69,326 rows; fold counts [14353, 12995, 14625, 12768, 14585]; sha1(sensor)[:12]=='f81d90085315'; sha1(stack[rec,start])[:12]=='e4177f2589d7'.
- train_window.py val_set reproduces exactly the same rows and limbs (same rng 1234+fold). Cell 14 asserts y and grp equality.

EXCLUSIONS / CORRECTIONS: none. There is no handling of sbj_10's last session, sbj_2's last 112 s or sbj_7's 10 s delay; labels are used as-is. The only subject-specific constant is TRAIN_SETS={0:2, 14:2} (subjects 0 and 14 did the circuit twice), used in count calibration.

OTHER TILINGS:
- Window-model training (cell 10) uses all stride-5 (0.1 s) windows with majority labels. One valid limb is drawn per sample per batch via torch.multinomial(W_ok).
- L0 scorer training tiles (cell 16, train_tiles): starts arange(0, T-50+1, 50) with the same video filter. limb rng = default_rng(42 + rec_idx*17), limb = min((rng.random(n)[:,None] > cp).sum(1), 3). Tiles without a valid limb are not dropped.
- L2 ridge statistics (cell 25, subject_stats): consecutive tile pairs (s, s+50) at STRIDE_B=10 samples, video +30 frames, all 4 limbs x 4 limbs. Video is unit-normalised and centred by the subject mean over the 1-s tiles.
--- cv_protocol:
One 5-fold subject split (sim_meta['fold'], cell 8): greedy balance by stride-5 window count, largest subjects first. train_window.py recomputes the identical split. Every stacking input for a training row is out-of-fold:
- Window log-probs come from the fold model that excluded the row's subject. Test gets the mean of the 5 fold models' log-softmax.
- L0 links on OOF rows come from fit_scorer trained on the recordings of other-fold subjects. Test uses a scorer trained on all 24 recordings.
- Ridge x{g} for L2 is fit on folds != g. The L2 training rows of fold g use RIDGES[g]; test averages log-odds over all 5 RIDGES.

There is no nested CV in this notebook; all settings are fixed to what the authors' nested CV chose. The tabular experts (S3, T) and the L2 link scorer are trained once on all 22 subjects and applied only to test. As a result there are NO OOF tabular predictions, NO OOF L2 links and NO OOF run of the graph stage here. The only full-pipeline OOF that can be computed is the cell-18 context baseline.

Seeds:
- Window runs: torch.manual_seed and np rng = seed*100+fold, with seeds 0-3 for fusion and 0-1 for IMU-only. Validation limb rng = 1234+fold.
- L0 scorer: seed 42, negatives rng 42.
- L2 negatives: rng 7000+sbj. LK_PARAMS seed 0, TAB_PARAMS seed 0.
--- window_models:

--- window_blend:
Cell 14 (W4):
- load_runs: OOF = mean_logp over runs, i.e. log of the mean probability. For test, each run's test_logp_raw (mean of fold log-softmax, so rows do not sum to 1) is first renormalised, then mean_logp is taken across runs.
- blend(fus, imu, wi=CFG['imu_w']=0.3) = log_softmax(fus_logp + 0.3*imu_logp), a weighted product of experts.
- Outputs: d['logp'] (OOF) and t['logp'] (test). fusion_logp and imu_logp are kept as separate keys.
- OOF macro F1 (hard-coded 'ours, 4F+2I'): fusion 0.7194, IMU-only 0.5995, blend 0.7253. Markdown table: 0.725.

Second-level test blend (cell 23): TEST_LOGP_B = tab_blend(t['logp'], [(S3, 0.5), (T, 0.3)]) = log_softmax(0.2*window_blend + 0.5*S3_logp + 0.3*T_logp). TAB_TEST_CHOICE = (('S3',0.5),('T',0.3)). This TEST_LOGP_B is P0 for the graph stage. Markdown OOF for this level: 0.877 (with L0 context + calibration).
--- graph_and_propagation:
(a) Context baseline (cell 4/18, diagnostic, OOF only). context_baseline(d) has three steps:
1. label_prop(exp(logp), emb, grp, *CFG['lp']=(k=10, tau=0.1, use_p=0.5, alpha=0.9), iters=20). Per subject: S = cos(emb) + 0.5*sqrt(P)sqrt(P)^T; kNN k=10; W = exp((s - smax)/0.1), symmetrised by max; symmetric normalisation; f <- 0.9*Wn f + 0.1*f0.
2. Row-normalise.
3. link_smooth(P, succ, score, alpha=0.5, iters=10) with weights sigmoid(L0 score).
Then finish(). Reference OOF 0.8473.

PLP (cell 20): plp_of = the same label_prop on the window blend, giving PLP_OOF and PLP_TEST. Used only as L2 link features (bc_lp, maxp_i, maxp_j).

(b) Final graph (cell 29). Test only: dt = dict(logp=TEST_LOGP_B, emb=t['emb'], grp=sbj, sbj, succ=L2_SUCC, score=L2_SCORE_QN, sets={}).

graph_P averages 5 TEST_CFGS:
- "g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r3w0.5"
- "g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5"
- "g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r2w0.7"
- "g0.5/abs0.6/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5"
- "g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r2w0.7"
These are "rs_combo 'comboqn' test top-5". Fields: g = emb_g, abs = link_mode 'abs' with beta, b = link bias, a = alpha, r = rounds, w = pseudo-label weight; T=2.0.

Runner(dd, K=40, b=-2.0):
- P0 = exp(logp).
- Lw = link_W: symmetric weight sigmoid(score + 2) on each L2 link.
- smooth_emb: E normalised, then E' = E + 0.5*row_norm(Lw) @ E (h=1).
- SubjectKNN(E', K=40): per subject, S = E'E'^T + use_p 0.5 * sqrt(P0)sqrt(P0)^T. The top-40 are cached and always built from P0 (Qsrc=P0 in later rounds).
- knn_W with k=10, tau=0.1: exp((v - vmax)/tau), symmetrised by max.
- Normalisation is norm='row'. The markdown implies sym, but kw forces 'row'.
- Link mixing: Al = row_norm(Lw sub-block); bb = beta*min(deg_link/2, 1) ('abs' mode).
- 20 iterations of f <- alpha*[(1-bb)*A f + bb*Al f] + (1-alpha)*f0.

Self-training: for rounds-1 extra rounds, Q = calibrate(norm_rows(F**2)) (Sinkhorn per subject), f0 = norm_rows(P0^(1-w) * Q^w), then propagate again with the same alpha. The configs' F are averaged and row-normalised to give P_TEST, saved as ROOT/P_test.npy.
--- links:
L0 (cell 16, adapted from honghanhhh):
- Candidates: union of top-k by tail3-vs-head3 cosine (_K_TH=40), last-vs-first frame (_K_11=15) and mean-vs-mean (_K_MP=15), computed on raw unit-normalised VideoMAE frames.
- 12 pair features: fwd, frame, pooled, rev, dom_r, dom_c, margin, log rank_row, log rank_col, same-limb, log IMU gap (last-to-first sample), log extrapolation gap. Gaps are NaN when the limb differs.
- Scorer: LGBMClassifier with 200 rounds, lr 0.05, leaves 31, min_child 40, subsample 0.8, colsample 0.8, lambda 1, class_weight balanced, seed 42. Each row keeps the true next tile + 4 random negatives.
- Assignment: l0_assign = Hungarian (linear_sum_assignment) on the log-odds cost; drop links with log-odds < -1.0; break each cycle at its weakest edge.
- OOF links per fold use a scorer trained on the other folds' recordings; test uses a scorer trained on all recordings.
- Hard-coded "ours" OOF: linked 0.924, exact 0.330, same-label 0.887. Assert: linked > 0.9 on OOF and test.
- Consumers: S3 neighbor_block and the context baseline.

L2 combined links ('all' variant; cells 24-27):
- A. Ridge boundary predictors (RIDGE_LAM=0.1, SHRINK=0.1). Predict the next window's head-3 video block from [Last, Tail, Head, Mean] blocks, and the reverse, using subject-centred unit frames. Per limb-pair Gaussian IMU head/tail regressions use IMU_LAM=1e-3. One ridge per held-out fold ('ours' r2 about 0.58).
- B. Candidates per subject: 11 SOURCES (th r40, lf r15, mm r15, lfc r15, imu r8, imu c5, extlin r25, extlin c10, lfz c30, ext2 r25, dual r25), KCAP=160 by n_src, plus ridge-score (rs) top KNEW=20 per ridge model.
- Features: 65 = BASE49 (OLD30 + NEW_DECODE minus the 4 CA features; stored as float16) + NEW16 (ridge log-likelihoods, dominance, rs, ranks, cosines, IMU log-densities). BASE49 includes bc_w (Bhattacharyya of sqrt window probs), bc_lp, maxp_i and maxp_j (from PLP), so the links depend on the window probabilities.
- C/D. One LightGBM scorer: objective cross_entropy, lr 0.05, leaves 63, min_data 100, bagging 0.8, feature_fraction 0.8, l2 1, 300 rounds. Trained on all 22 train subjects; fold-g rows use RIDGES[g]. Each row keeps the true successor + LK_NEG=16 random negatives. 'Ours': 1,173,696 rows, positive rate 0.0549, candidate recall 0.930.
- E. Test: mean log-odds over the 5 ridge variants; lk_assign (Hungarian, drop edges <= floor+1 = -49, cycle breaking). Then qnorm: per-subject rank -> quantile of the fixed QN_REF 1001-point table of their OOF linked scores. Reason given: "test link scores are shifted low (mostly subject 22)".
- 'Ours': test linked 0.951, raw median score 0.52. No exact-successor accuracy is reported for L2.

Outputs: ROOT/links_L0.npz and ROOT/links_L2_test.npz. There are no OOF L2 links.
--- counts_constraint:
calibrate() (cell 4) runs per subject Sinkhorn with 50 iterations:
- null = max(n - 18*per_ex*sets, null_min*n), with per_ex=97 and null_min=0.05.
- Each exercise column target = (n - null)/18; column 0 target = null.
- Alternate column scaling and row normalisation.
- sets = TRAIN_SETS {0:2, 14:2} for OOF and {} for test, so every test subject is treated as doing the circuit once.

It is used twice:
- Inside graph self-training (Runner.cal with T=2.0, i.e. F**2 normalised, then calibrate), to build the pseudo-label targets.
- In finish(): Q = clip(P, 1e-12)^(1/sharpen_T=0.5), i.e. squared, then normalised, then calibrate, then argmax.

Markdown rationale: "Every subject performs each of the 18 exercises once, which is about 97 one-second windows each; the rest is null." 'Ours' predicted null share per test subject: 22 0.66, 23 0.43, 24 0.15, 25 0.09. There is no contiguity or segment constraint; counts are enforced only via Sinkhorn soft marginals.
--- refiner:
None. There is no boundary refiner, Viterbi/HMM or chain-level segment decoding. The final stage (cell 29) is: graph_P (5-config average) -> finish (sharpen T=0.5, per-subject Sinkhorn to 97/exercise, argmax). The submission merges labels onto sample_submission ids and writes WORK/submission.csv; cell 30 prints stage times and deletes ROOT when CLEANUP.

--- plug_in_point:
WHERE WINDOW PROBABILITIES ENTER
Cell 14 (W4) builds two dicts: d (OOF, 69,326 rows) and t (test, 12,234 rows). Every later stage reads only these keys:
- 'logp': (N,19) natural-log, row-normalised log-probs (the window blend).
- 'fusion_logp' and 'imu_logp': (N,19), used only as S3 features.
- 'emb': (N,768) float32 video embedding.
- 'succ' and 'score': added later by L0 in cell 16.

ROW ORDER
- d rows follow ROOT/sim_meta.npz order: fold 0..4, then rec index (the sorted(train/inertial_feat/*.csv) order, PROC/recordings.json), then start ascending.
- Only tiles with start % 50 == 0 are present, with limb sim['sensor'] (0 right_arm, 1 right_leg, 2 left_leg, 3 left_arm).
- t rows follow test_meta_data.csv ROW order (PROC/test_id.npy), NOT necessarily sorted id order.
- Our test (12234,19) in test-id order must be re-indexed: pos = {id: i for i, id in enumerate(our_ids)}; ext_t = ext[[pos[x] for x in np.load(PROC/'test_id.npy', allow_pickle=True)]].

MAPPING OUR STRIDE-25 OOF (n_win,4,19) TO d ROWS
- For row r: stem = recs[sim['rec'][r]]['stem'] and s = sim['start'][r]. s is a multiple of 50, so it lies on our stride-25 grid. Look up our window (stem, s), then take [sensor = sim['sensor'][r], :].
- Permute our limb axis if its order differs from SENSORS.
- Check full coverage of the 69,326 rows. Their filter is s*3//5 + 8 + 15 <= F and at least one valid limb.
- Check that our majority labels equal sim['y'].
- Convert with np.log(np.clip(p, 1e-8, 1)) and renormalise.

WHAT CONSUMES THEM
d['logp']:
- cell 18 context_baseline (OOF diagnostic);
- cell 20 PLP_OOF, which becomes L2 features bc_lp, maxp_i and maxp_j;
- cell 23 S3 features, including neighbor_block (linked-neighbour logp/ctx) and knn_block;
- cell 26 subject_all bc_w (L2 scorer training).
d['fusion_logp'] and d['imu_logp']: S3 only.
t['logp']:
- cell 20 PLP_TEST;
- cell 23 S3 test features and the 0.2 share in tab_blend;
- cell 27 subject_all bc_w, which drives the test L2 links.
TEST_LOGP_B (end of cell 23) becomes dt['logp'], i.e. the graph P0, in cell 29.

IS OOF REQUIRED?
YES for:
- S3 training (cell 23: booster trained on tab_build('S3', d, 'oof') with y=d['y']);
- L2 link-scorer training (cell 26);
- the cell-18 diagnostic.
NO for: T, L0 links, ridges, graph propagation and finish. These use only video, IMU, emb, or test-side probabilities.

Scene-cluster folds are acceptable only if each subject's rows were predicted by a model that never saw that subject. Check subjects 0 and 14, which have two recordings each. There is no weight tuning or count learning on OOF in this notebook. TAB_TEST_CHOICE, TAB_ROUNDS, TEST_CFGS and CFG are all fixed constants.

OPTION 1 (consistent, needs OOF)
- At the end of cell 14 (before cell 16), set d['logp'] = log_softmax((1-lam)*d['logp'] + lam*log(ext_oof)), and do the same for t with ext_t.
- Optionally add d['ext_logp'] and t['ext_logp'] and append them in tab_build (cell 23) next to [dd['logp'], dd['fusion_logp'], dd['imu_logp']]. Change the shape assert from 533 to 552.
- Tune lam on OOF with macro_f1(d['y'], finish(context_baseline(d), d)) after cell 16 (reference 0.8473). This is the only full-OOF metric available.

OPTION 2 (GPU-free)
- Skip cell 12 (W3) and in cell 14 set fusion_logp = our NN, imu_logp = our LightGBM and logp = our blend.
- Recompute emb on CPU exactly as vid_emb in train_window.py:
  - per train subject, VM/VS = mean/std(+1e-3) of float32 frames vid[::3] over that subject's recordings;
  - per test subject, use all its test frames reshaped to (-1,768);
  - emb = mean over the 15 frames of (v - VM[sbj]) / VS[sbj], using sim_vid.npy for d and test_vid.npy for t (stored as f16 originally).
- Drop the cell-14 asserts that read FUSION[0].
- The whole notebook then becomes CPU-only, about 30 min of CPU stages.

OPTION 3 (test-only, no OOF)
- In cell 29, before dt = dict(logp=TEST_LOGP_B, ...), set TEST_LOGP_B = log_softmax((1-lam)*TEST_LOGP_B + lam*log(ext_t)).
- lam cannot be validated here, because the graph stage runs only on test.

PERSISTING INTERMEDIATES
Set WEAR_CLEANUP=0 and WEAR_ROOT=/kaggle/working/... to keep P_test.npy, test_logp_b.npy and the links for cross-kernel blending. Delete the large proc/*_vid.npy and sim_vid.npy first (about 6.5 GB of scratch).

--- bugs_or_risks:
[
"kernel-metadata enable_gpu=false while cell 1 requires GPU T4 x2. A fork run as-is takes the CPU_RUNS fallback (4 epochs x 100 steps x bs 256 per fold) and gets much weaker window models. This likely explains why the title says LB 0.890 and the markdown says 0.903 (inference)",
"CLEANUP=True on Kaggle deletes ROOT at the end. No P_test, test_logp_b, links or OOF arrays survive as kernel outputs unless WEAR_CLEANUP=0",
"No OOF evaluation of the final pipeline: S3/T and the L2 scorer are trained once and applied to test only. Any change to probabilities, blend weights or TEST_CFGS can only be checked through the cell-18 context-baseline proxy or the LB",
"S3 and the L2 scorer learn from OOF-window-prob features (logp, fusion_logp, imu_logp, bc_w, bc_lp, maxp). Replacing only test probabilities (or OOF and test probabilities that are calibrated differently) shifts the feature distribution. Test probabilities are already a 5-fold geometric mean while OOF probabilities come from single models",
"Markdown cell 28 implies symmetric kNN normalisation, but Runner passes norm='row'. The code is authoritative",
"No label corrections or exclusions (sbj_10 last session, sbj_2 last 112 s, sbj_7 10 s delay are not handled). Majority-label ties go to the lowest class id (null)",
"Count prior is hard-wired to per_ex=97 and one set per test subject. If a test subject repeated or skipped exercises, Sinkhorn forces wrong marginals. 'Ours' null share for subject 22 is 0.66, so most of its windows are pushed to null",
"The cell-14 assert (_o['grp'] == sim['sbj']) relies on train subject ids being contiguous 0..21 (grp is the subject index)",
"Memory: many per-subject (n,n) float32/int32/float64 matrices in L0 pair_features, L2 subject_all, ll_mats and ranks_at. The largest subjects (0 and 14, two sets each) can push RAM high on Kaggle CPU",
"GPU nondeterminism, fp16 on T4 vs the original bf16, and BLAS thread differences: authors expect +-0.003 LB variance. The L2 negative-sampling RNG alone changes about 2% of test labels"
]

--- compute:
GPU notebook. Cell 0: "competition data + GPU T4 x2 + Run All (~1.5–2 h)". Cell 1 checks for a usable GPU in a subprocess. Without one it falls back to CPU_RUNS (4 epochs x 100 steps, "score will be far below the LB target"). BLAS threads are set to 4 ("Kaggle has 4 vCPUs"). WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN = 150 (env WEAR_WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN) caps wall time for the window-model GPU stage. Scratch ROOT is /tmp/wear when /tmp has more than 12e9 bytes free ("about 6.5 GB of scratch"), otherwise WORK/_wear. CLEANUP (default 1 on Kaggle) runs rmtree on ROOT in cell 26. With 2 GPUs, the window runs are split into two longest-first balanced queues, one per GPU, using threads and subprocesses, with a 30 s stagger for "the 3.2 GB data loads". train_window.py puts all train ACC and VID on the GPU. train_link.py runs folds "0,2,4" on GPU0 and "1,3" on GPU1, one seed at a time. The L2 Gram accumulation also uses the GPU. A stage() context manager logs time and RSS, but nothing was logged, so there is no runtime or memory evidence.
