--- tile_construction:
Cell 4, oof_windows(). The resulting table is saved as ROOT/sim_meta.npz with keys rec, start, sensor, y, sbj, fold, plus sim_acc.npy (N,50,3) float32 and sim_vid.npy (N,15,768) float16.
1. For each recording i (index = position in sorted(train/inertial_feat/*.csv)), take candidate starts = np.arange(0, T-50+1, STRIDE=5). Keep a start only if start*3//5 + 8 + 15 <= F, i.e. the video frames exist.
2. Label = majority (argmax of class counts over samples [start, start+50); ties go to the lower class index). There is no center label.
3. W_ok (n,4): a limb is valid if no NaN in its 3 axes over the window. The valid mask is computed BEFORE NaN interpolation, which is pandas interpolate(limit_direction='both').fillna(0). Windows with no valid limb are dropped.
4. Folds: 5 folds by subject. Subjects are sorted by descending stride-5 window count, and each is assigned to the currently least-loaded fold.
5. For fold = 0..4: idx = windows of that fold with start % 50 == 0, i.e. non-overlapping 1 s tiles on a grid aligned to recording sample 0. Limb: rng = np.random.default_rng(1234 + fold); cp = cumsum(W_ok/W_ok.sum(1)); sensor = (rng.random(len(idx))[:,None] > cp).sum(1), which is uniform over valid limbs.
6. Row order: fold 0 rows, then fold 1, ... fold 4. Within a fold, rows are in ascending (recording index, start).
7. Limb codes: SENSORS = ['right_arm','right_leg','left_leg','left_arm'] map to 0..3.
8. Video frames for a tile: (start*3//5) + 8 + arange(15), i.e. frames 8..22 of the second. IMU: acc[start:start+50, sensor].
Hard asserts (cell 4): len = 69326; per-fold counts [14353, 12995, 14625, 12768, 14585]; sha1(sensor)[:12] == 'f81d90085315'; sha1(stack[rec, start])[:12] == 'e4177f2589d7'. Cell 3 asserts 24 recordings, sum T = 3466400, sum F = 2079840, F*5 == T*3.
TRAIN_SETS = {0: 2, 14: 2} (cell 2): "train subjects 0 and 14 recorded the exercise circuit twice". This only feeds the count prior.
There are NO label corrections or exclusions. The code has nothing for sbj_10's last session, sbj_2's last 112 s, or sbj_7's 10 s delay.
Separately, the L0 link scorer uses train_tiles() (cell 12): starts arange(0, T-50+1, 50) with the same video filter, limb rng = default_rng(seed + i*17) with seed = 42, and limbs = np.minimum(..., 3).
train_link.py builds its training batches with a random phase in 0..49 and random valid limbs.
--- cv_protocol:
One fold map, fold_of (subject -> fold, from cell 4's greedy balanced split), is used everywhere:
- train_window.py recomputes the same split.
- Cell 7 asserts that OOF y and grp equal sim y and sbj, which also implies train subject ids are 0..21 = SB index.
- train_link.py reads fold_of from sim_meta.
- L0 scorer, L2 ridges, L3 scorers, tabular nested OOF, count model, adapt_T and the refine null model all use it.

Seeds:
- OOF limb choice: 1234 + fold.
- Window nets: torch/np seed = seed*100 + fold, seeds 0..3 for fusion and 0..1 for IMU-only.
- Two-tower: seed*100 + fold, LINK_SEEDS [0, 1].
- L0 tiles: 42 + i*17. L0 negatives: rng 42.
- L2/L3 negative sampling: default_rng(7000 + sbj).
- Gumbel bagging: 10000*ti + 1000*k + sbj.
- adapt_T halves: seed k for OOF fold k, seed 99 for test.
- LightGBM: seed 0 (TAB/LK params), random_state 42 (L0).

Nesting:
- TAB_OOF (S3 and T) is leave-one-fold-out (cell 15).
- L3 OOF links are leave-one-fold-out (oof_links, cell 22). The L2 ridge for subject s uses RIDGES[fold(s)], fitted on the other 4 folds.
- Counts: profile features are leave-one-fold-out per fold (cell 24).
- Refine null model is leave-one-fold-out.
- Not nested: LINK_MODEL (the L2 scorer, cell 17) is trained on all OOF subjects and only applied to test. With ABLATION=1, cell 22 additionally computes nested L2 OOF links for the "public pipeline" score.

Test-time models are always refit on all 22 subjects / 69326 tiles.
--- window_models:
[
{
"name": "final_s0..final_s3 (fusion Net, train_window.py, cell 5/6)",
"modality": "IMU (1 limb, 50x3 + magnitude) + video (15x768) + limb embedding",
"details": "IMU branch: dilated ConvBlocks 36->64->128->128->256->256, mean+max pooled. Video branch: Dropout(0.5) -> Linear(768, 256) -> mean+std pooling ('pool' arch). Fuse MLP plus aux IMU and video heads (aux loss weight 0.3). Training samples: all stride-5 windows of the training-fold subjects, with class-frequency^-0.5 sampling and a random valid limb per sample. Augmentation: random rotation up to 20 deg, scale 0.9-1.1, noise 0.02; video noise 0.1, 10% frame drop, 20% no video, 10% no IMU. Optimisation: label smoothing 0.1, AdamW lr 1e-3 wd 0.05, OneCycle (pct 0.1), EMA 0.999, bs 512, 300 steps/epoch, 12 epochs, dim 256. TRAIN_COMMON: --vnorm subject --anorm subject --vid_arch pool. Test subjects are normalised by their own test statistics (transductive). Outputs per run: oof_raw.npz (logp, y, grp, emb), test_logp_raw.npy (mean over 5 fold models of log-softmax, not renormalised), test_emb.npy. The knn smoothing and per-class bias inside the script are logging only and not used downstream.",
"oof_score": "Not logged. Code comment (cell 7) gives the author's value for fusion x4: 0.7194 window-level OOF macro F1."
},
{
"name": "imu_s0, imu_s1 (Net with --only imu)",
"modality": "IMU only",
"details": "Same script with video zeroed, 30 epochs, seeds 0 and 1.",
"oof_score": "Code comment: IMU-only x2 0.5995."
},
{
"name": "Tabular expert S3 (LightGBM multiclass, cells 14-15)",
"modality": "Hand IMU features + video PCA/motion + window log-probs + L0-link neighbour block + video kNN block",
"details": "Features (533 cols, asserted): imu_feats 130 + per (subject x limb) z-score 130 + sensor 1; vmot 4; vpca 96 (48 PCA comps of subject-centred frame mean + 48 temporal stds); d['logp'], fusion_logp, imu_logp (57); neighbor_block 94 (logp of the L0 successor/predecessor, 4-hop context mean, link scores, limbs, 16 z-IMU feats of each neighbour); knn_block 21 (k=10 video-cosine neighbours' log mean prob, max/mean similarity). TAB_PARAMS: lr 0.08, 31 leaves, min_data_in_leaf 80, feature_fraction 0.3, bagging 0.8, lambda_l2 5, max_bin 63. Rounds: S3 60. Test model is fit on all OOF rows; TAB_OOF is leave-one-fold-out.",
"oof_score": "Printed as 'S3: nested OOF macro F1' but no output exists."
},
{
"name": "Tabular expert T (LightGBM multiclass)",
"modality": "Hand IMU + video features only (no model probabilities)",
"details": "361 cols (asserted) = imu 261 + vmot 4 + vpca 96. Rounds 113. Also re-fit as 'adapted T' in cell 25: cross-fit on labelled train tiles plus pseudo-labelled new tiles (pseudo = argmax of decode A; PS_WEIGHT 3.0, PS_ROUNDS 150, min_data_in_leaf 40).",
"oof_score": "Not logged."
}
]
--- window_blend:
Stage 1 (cell 7):
- load_runs averages runs in probability space (mean_logp). For test, each run's test_logp_raw is first re-log-softmaxed.
- d['logp'] = blend(fus, imu, CFG['imu_w'] = 0.3) = log_softmax(fus_logp + 0.3*imu_logp), a product of experts rather than a convex mix.
- Code comment gives window-level OOF macro F1 "ours with 4F+2I: 0.7194 / 0.5995 / 0.7253" for fusion / IMU / blend.

Stage 2, "blend B" (cell 15):
- tab_blend(lp, parts) = log_softmax((1 - sum w)*lp + sum w_v*lgb_logp_v) with TAB_TEST_CHOICE = (('S3', 0.5), ('T', 0.3)). The window log-prob therefore gets weight 0.2.
- TEST_LOGP_B uses the test models; OOF_LOGP_B uses the leave-one-fold-out TAB_OOF. The printed nested OOF for both is not logged.
- Cell 15 also prints the cheap proxy "+ label propagation + L0 link smoothing + calibration" = finish(context_baseline(...)). Not logged.

Stage 3 (cell 25):
- B2 = tab_blend(d['logp'], [(TAB_OOF['S3'], 0.5), (TA_OOF, 0.3)]), i.e. T is replaced by the pseudo-label adapted T. Test is the same with TAB_TEST['S3'] and TA_TEST.
--- graph_and_propagation:
Embedding:
- d['emb'] / t['emb'] (N,768) = vid_emb from run final_s0: the mean over the 15 frames of per-subject-normalised VideoMAE features, with (v - VM[subject]) / VS[subject].
- VM/VS for train subjects come from every 3rd frame of all their recordings; for test subjects, from all of their test tile frames.
- There is NO PC removal.

Plp (cell 13):
- label_prop(exp(logp), emb, sbj, k=10, tau=0.1, use_p=0.5, alpha=0.9, iters=20). Per subject, S = cos(E) + 0.5*sqrt(P) sqrt(P)^T, top-10 kNN, weights exp((s - max)/0.1), symmetrised by max, then symmetric normalisation and f = 0.9*W f + 0.1*f0.
- Used only as link pair features (bc_lp, maxp_i, maxp_j).

Main graph (cell 19): graph_P(dd, TEST_CFGS) averages 5 configs:
- 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r3w0.5'
- 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5'
- 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r2w0.7'
- 'g0.5/abs0.6/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5'
- 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r2w0.7'

Config codes: g = emb_g, abs = link_mode with beta, b = link bias, a = alpha, r = rounds, w = pseudo weight.

Runner(dd, K=40, b=-2.0):
- Link matrix Lw = symmetric link_W with weights sigmoid((score - b)/1).
- Embedding smoothed along the links: E + 0.5*rownorm(Lw) E.
- SubjectKNN: top-40 by cos + 0.5*sqrt(P0) sqrt(P0)^T; k=10 and tau=0.1 are used, with row-normalised kNN W.
- label_prop_generic, 20 iters: g = (1 - bb)*A f + bb*rownorm(Lw) f, where bb = beta*min(deg_link/2, 1) ('abs'); then f = alpha*g + (1 - alpha)*f0.
- Self-training for rounds - 1 passes: Q = Sinkhorn(norm(F^2)) using fixed 97/class/set or learned targets, then re-propagate P0^(1-w)*Q^w with the kNN graph still built from P0.

finish():
- Q = P^(1/0.5) (sharpen_T 0.5, so squared), normalised, then a per-subject Sinkhorn calibrate with 50 iters.
- Each exercise column gets per_ex = 97 x sets. null = max(n - 18*97*sets, 0.05*n).
- Labels = argmax.

Baseline (cell 19): dt with logp = TEST_LOGP_B, L2 links, qnorm'd scores, fed through graph_P, then finish, written to submission_baseline.csv.

Decode A/B (cell 24): bag_P runs graph_P for each of the 15 link matchings and takes the geometric mean.

context_baseline (cell 2, using L0 links + link_smooth alpha 0.5, 10 iters) is used only for the cell 15 logging metric.
--- links:
L0 (cell 12):
- pair_features with 12 features: fwd tail(3)·head(3) cosine, last·first frame, mean·mean, reverse, row/col dominance, margin, log ranks, same limb, and IMU gap/extrapolation for same-limb pairs.
- Candidates: union of top-k with _K_TH 40, _K_11 15, _K_MP 15.
- LGBMClassifier with 200 rounds, lr 0.05, 31 leaves, class_weight balanced, 4 negatives per tile. Trained on train_tiles; OOF per fold; test model on all recordings.
- l0_assign: Hungarian (linear_sum_assignment), drop log-odds < -1.0, break each cycle at its weakest link.
- Saved as links_L0.npz. Asserts linked fraction > 0.9 for both OOF and test.
- Feeds S3's neighbor_block.

L2 ridge boundary predictors (cell 16):
- Per-subject sufficient statistics over consecutive pairs (s, s+50) at STRIDE_B = 10.
- Video blocks [First, Head3, Tail3, Last, Mean] of subject-centred unit frames.
- Forward ridge X(i) -> head(j) and backward ridge X(j) -> tail(i), RIDGE_LAM 0.1, Gaussian residual precision with SHRINK 0.1.
- Per limb pair IMU linear-Gaussian, IMU_LAM 1e-3.
- RIDGES[g] is fit on folds != g.

L2 scorer (cell 17):
- subject_all builds candidates from 11 SOURCES: th r40, lf r15, mm r15, lfc r15, imu r8, imu c5, extlin r25, extlin c10, lfz c30, ext2 r25, dual r25. Capped at KCAP 160 by source count, plus the ridge-score (rs) top KNEW = 20 per model.
- Features: BASE49 (OLD30 + NEW_DECODE minus CA_FEATS), stored float16, plus NEW16 ridge features (ll_f/ll_b, dominance, rs, ranks, cos_f/cos_b, raw_f, imu_f/imu_b), for 65 in total.
- Probability-dependent features: bc_w = Bhattacharyya of window probs exp(d['logp']); bc_lp, maxp_i, maxp_j from Plp.
- LINK_MODEL: LightGBM cross_entropy, 300 rounds, lr 0.05, 63 leaves, min_data_in_leaf 100. Rows = true successor + LK_NEG = 16 random negatives per tile, over all OOF subjects.
- Test (cell 18): mean log-odds over the 5 RIDGES variants, then lk_assign (Hungarian, drop edges at floor, cycle breaking), then qnorm. qnorm maps each subject's link scores by rank onto QN_REF, a hard-coded 1001-point quantile table from the author's pooled OOF.
- Comment: test linked fraction "(ours 0.951)", raw median score "(ours 0.52)".
- Pair files ROOT/pairs_{s}.npz and testpairs_{s}.npz hold cand, valid, base, new.

Two-tower (train_link.py, cells 9-10):
- Tail and head Tower (video GRU + IMU conv edge features + same-limb block of size 48).
- Score = scale*cos(g_i, h_j) + 1[same limb]<gs_i, hs_j>, with learned log_scale initialised at log 20.
- In-batch successor and predecessor cross-entropy. 3000 steps, R = 2 recordings x M = 512 tiles per step (two contiguous blocks), lr 1e-3, EMA 0.999, per fold.
- Logs "held-out top-1 successor accuracy" (not available).
- Writes emb_sim_f{g}.npz (idx, G, H) and emb_test_f{g}.npz for seeds [0, 1].

L3 (cells 21-22):
- Adds 22 boundary features to the 65: tower 8 (log-softmax row/col both directions + dominance/ranks, averaged over embeddings: OOF 2 seeds, test 2 seeds x 5 folds); ssl 7 (per-subject ridge first<->last frame, lam 0.01); soft bout 7 (sqrt-prob agreement, both-null, both-exercise, argmax agree, emb cosine, margins). Soft bout uses d['logp'] / t['logp'] and emb. Total 87 features.
- oof_links trains leave-one-fold-out L3 models, then L3_MODEL on all rows. Test averages log-odds over 5 ridge variants, then lk_assign and qnorm.
- Prints linked / exact / same-label and a gain share by block. None of it is logged.

Bagging (cell 23):
- BAG_K = 8, BAG_TAUS = [0.2, 0.3]. Gives 1 unperturbed + 7 Gumbel-perturbed matchings per tau = 15 link sets, each with L + tau*Gumbel, reassigned and qnorm'd.
--- counts_constraint:
Fixed prior (cell 2, calibrate):
- Per-subject Sinkhorn, 50 iters.
- Each exercise class target = per_ex 97 x sets, where sets = TRAIN_SETS {0: 2, 14: 2} on train and {} (so 1) on test.
- null = max(n - 18*97*sets, null_min 0.05*n). The remaining mass is spread evenly over the 18 classes.
- Applied after sharpening P^2 (sharpen_T 0.5).

Learned prior (cell 24, decode()), COUNT_PASSES = 3:
- profile_features([P, Bp]) gives one row per (subject, class 1..18). For each of the 2 matrices (graph-bagged P and the blend B probs): 9 order-statistics of the sorted log(q_c) - log(max other) at ranks RK = [60, 70, 80, 90, 97, 105, 115, 125, 140] x sets; plus q.sum/sets, argmax count/sets, and counts of sc > 0, > -1, > 1 (/sets).
- Extra features: run-length (longest, n_runs, total) of argmax labels of P; link consistency (fraction of class-c tiles whose L3 successor is also c); n/sets; null share. That is 34 features.
- Target: true count / sets.
- fit_counts trains only on rows with 55 < target < 150. Features are z-scored. Model: RidgeCV(alphas [1, 3, 10, 30, 100, 300, 1000]) plus LightGBM regression (lr 0.05, 15 leaves, min_data 8, 220 rounds). Prediction = 0.55*ridge + 0.45*lgb, clipped to [70, 135].
- OOF counts are leave-one-fold-out over subject folds; test counts come from a model fit on all.
- count_targets: null = max(n - sum(per*sets), 0.05 n). Classes are rescaled to fill n - null.
- Between passes, P and Pt are recomputed with bag_P(..., targets), so the self-training Sinkhorn inside the Runner also uses the targets.
- Final: finish_targets (P^2, then calibrate_targets).
- Logs "count error" against "fixed 97". Not available.
--- refiner:
Pseudo-label adapt T (cell 25):
- Pseudo-labels = QA_OOF / QA_TEST argmax from decode A.
- adapt_T cross-fits two random halves. Each half trains LightGBM T on labelled train tiles plus the other half's pseudo-labelled tiles, weighted PS_WEIGHT 3.0, with PS_PARAMS = TAB_PARAMS + min_data_in_leaf 40, for PS_ROUNDS 150.
- OOF: per fold k, labelled = other folds.
- B2 then goes through decode stage B (the full bag + count passes again).

Boundary refine, refine_boundary (cell 25):
- Tile features (10): P[:,0], margin, top1, top2, is_null, link_break (label differs from L3 successor), link score, successor null prob, cosine to successor, null rate among 10 video neighbours.
- LightGBM binary null-vs-exercise (lr 0.05, 31 leaves, min_data 40, 200 rounds), leave-one-fold-out OOF plus an all-data test model.
- apply_flip on post-Sinkhorn Q: exercise -> null if null_prob >= REFINE_NULL_HI 0.65 and margin <= REFINE_MARGIN 0.18; null -> best exercise if null_prob <= REFINE_NULL_LO 0.35 and margin <= 0.18. The forced margin is +0.05, followed by renormalisation with no re-Sinkhorn.
- Gate: applied only if OOF macro F1 improves by at least 1e-4. Otherwise it prints "GATE FAIL".

Final labels: QB_TEST argmax, written to submission.csv.

--- plug_in_point:
Where: cell 7 (stage 'W4 blend'), right after the dicts d (OOF) and t (test) are built and before 'del _o, ...'. Every later stage reads d['logp'] / t['logp'], so overriding them there propagates everywhere:
- Plp (cell 13)
- S3 features and tab_blend (cell 15)
- L2 bc_w (cell 17-18)
- Soft bout features (cell 21)
- Decode A/B via OOF_LOGP_B / TEST_LOGP_B / B2 (cells 15, 24, 25)

The L0 links (cell 12) and the T expert use no probabilities.

Required layouts:
(1) OOF: an array (69326,19) of log-probs (rows log-softmax-normalised) in sim row order. Row order is fold 0..4 (notebook folds), then ascending (rec, start), with rec = index in sorted(INPUT/train/inertial_feat/*.csv). Build it from your (n_win, 4, 19) stride-25 OOF:
- key[(rec, start)] -> your window index.
- rows = [key[(r, s)] for r, s in zip(sim['rec'], sim['start'])].
- P = ext_oof[rows, sim['sensor']].
- Limb axis must follow SENSORS = ['right_arm', 'right_leg', 'left_leg', 'left_arm'].
- All sim starts are multiples of 50, so they lie on your stride-25 grid. Check that all 69326 (rec, start) pairs exist in your index and that your recording order matches the sorted csv stems.
(2) Test: (12234,19) in test_meta_data.csv row order, not necessarily sorted by id. Reindex with ids = np.load(PROC/'test_id.npy', allow_pickle=True): pos = pd.Index(ext_ids).get_indexer(ids); assert (pos >= 0).all(); P_t = ext_test[pos].

Options:
(a) Replace: d['logp'] = log(clip(P_ext_oof)) renormalised; t['logp'] likewise.
(b) Blend, recommended: d['logp'] = log_softmax((1-lam)*d['logp'] + lam*L_ext), and the same for t, using the same lam. Tune lam on macro_f1(d['y'], d['logp'].argmax(1)), and preferably on the cheap cell 15 proxy finish(context_baseline({**d, 'logp': OOF_LOGP_B}), d).
- d['fusion_logp'] / d['imu_logp'] (and t's) are extra S3 inputs. To keep the hard assert X.shape == (69326, 533) in cell 15, swap them for your NN and LightGBM log-probs at equal width, or leave them unchanged. If you add columns, edit the 533 assert.
(c) Lowest intrusion: keep d/t untouched and add an extra tab expert. Append (EXT_OOF, w) to the parts lists in cell 15 (TEST_LOGP_B with EXT_TEST, OOF_LOGP_B with EXT_OOF) and in cell 25 (B2_OOF/B2_TEST), shrinking the window weight. tab_blend computes (1 - sum w)*lp + sum w_v*q_v.

Is train-tile OOF required? Yes, for any consistent use. The OOF copy trains:
- the S3 expert (cell 15)
- the L2/L3 link scorers through bc_w and soft-bout features (cells 17, 22)
- the learned count model (cell 24 profile_features on OOF P/Bp vs true counts)
- adapt_T pseudo-labels (cell 25)
- the refine null model and its gate (cell 25)
Without OOF you can only inject into the test side of the final tab_blend (TEST_LOGP_B and B2_TEST). The count model and refine would then be trained on a mismatched distribution, so that is risky.

Your OOF must be genuinely subject-held-out, and its calibration should resemble your test probabilities. The notebook's own test logp is a 5-fold-model average while its OOF is single-model. Scene-cluster folds are acceptable only if no model scoring a subject saw that subject. Nested scores here use the notebook's folds, not yours.

Skipping the window training (cell 6, GPU time) is possible, but cell 7 still needs d['emb'] / t['emb'], which come from FUSION[0]. Recompute them as the frame mean of (vid - VM[subject]) / VS[subject]. For train subjects, VM/VS = mean / std(+1e-3) of float32 of every 3rd frame of all that subject's recordings. For each test subject, use all of its test frames. Also remove the cell 6 and 7 asserts on FUSION and IMU.

--- bugs_or_risks:
[
"No outputs are saved in the notebook, so none of the v2 stage scores or the v2 LB can be verified. The title says 'LB 0.9'; the markdown says v1 got 0.92485 and v2 is a 'target'.",
"The dataset jiweiliu/wear-timeline-graph-models is attached but never read. Nothing is precomputed; the whole pipeline (window nets, two-tower, L0-L3, counts) is retrained in roughly 1.5-2 h on T4 x2.",
"The time guard (WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN 150) can silently skip final_s1..s3 / imu_s1, so ensemble size and score can vary between runs. Only final_s0 and imu_s0 are asserted.",
"OOF vs test calibration mismatch: test window logp is a mean over 5 fold models (x seeds), while OOF comes from single fold models. All OOF-trained downstream stages (S3, bc_w and soft-bout link features, count model, refine) are fit on less sharp inputs than they see at test time.",
"L3 test log-odds are averaged over 5 ridge variants, while OOF uses one ridge per subject. qnorm against the hard-coded QN_REF (the author's OOF distribution) masks score-scale drift.",
"LINK_MODEL (L2) is trained on all OOF subjects with no nesting. That is only a problem for the baseline/L2 test links; L3 is nested.",
"refine_boundary edits post-Sinkhorn probabilities without re-calibration and is gated by a single OOF comparison (+1e-4), so the gate is a small optimistic selection.",
"Count model: training only uses rows with 55 < true count < 150, and predictions are clipped to [70, 135], so subjects with unusual counts are pulled toward the norm.",
"No label corrections are applied (nothing for sbj_10, sbj_2 or sbj_7 timing or label issues), and the window label is the majority over the 50 samples.",
"oof_windows limb sampling has no np.minimum(..., 3) guard, unlike train_tiles. The sha1 assert pins the expected result, so any data change will trip the asserts.",
"Hard shape asserts (69326 rows, 533/361 tabular columns, fold sizes, hashes) break as soon as feature widths change, e.g. when adding external probabilities as extra S3 columns.",
"train_link.py calls torch.amp.GradScaler('cuda', ...) with no fallback, unlike train_window.py's make_scaler.",
"CLEANUP deletes ROOT, so intermediate link and pair arrays are lost. Re-decoding with new probabilities needs a full rerun unless WEAR_CLEANUP=0 and the key arrays are copied to /kaggle/working.",
"_link_consistency has an unused argument (idx_local). This is harmless."
]
--- improvement_ideas:
[
"Main lever: geometric-blend our NN+LGBM per-window probabilities into d['logp'] / t['logp'] at cell 7, using log_softmax((1-lam)*nb + lam*ext), with sim-order and meta-order mapping as described in plug_in_point. Our standalone LB is 0.785, while this notebook's window blend OOF comment is 0.7253, so our probabilities should strengthen Plp, S3, bc_w, soft-bout, decode and counts. Tune lam on window-level OOF F1 and on the cheap cell 15 proxy (blend B + LP + L0 smoothing + calibration) before paying for the full decode.",
"If OOF compatibility is doubtful, use the lower-risk variant: add ext as a third expert in tab_blend (cells 15 and 25), e.g. (('S3', 0.45), ('T', 0.25), ('EXT', 0.2)), so the link stages stay unchanged.",
"Feed our probabilities as S3 inputs by swapping them in for fusion_logp / imu_logp at equal width, which keeps the 533 assert valid, so the stacker learns how much to trust them.",
"Make reruns cheap: set WEAR_CLEANUP=0 and save to /kaggle/working the arrays d['logp'], d['emb'], t['emb'], TAB_OOF, TAB_TEST, XT_OOF, XT_TEST, L3_OOF_LINKS, L3_TEST_LINKS, sim_meta. NOTEBOOK: kansukehabano/what-egocentric-video-embeddings-encode, "What Egocentric Video Embeddings Encode" (Kaggle id 137799506, author handle habakan). Converted text: /Users/randy/Desktop/Kaggle/wear/ref2/wha
LB: Public LB 0.894 for the final submission (markdown cells 2 and 20). The paper configuration (no count band, lambda=4) scored public LB 0.871 [repo README]. CV for the final config is 0.886 (three seeds) per cell 20; the repo README gives 0.8688 -> 0.8862 over three seeds for adding the count band.
--- lineage:
Original work by habakan (Kaggle kansukehabano), accompanying their paper "What egocentric video embeddings encode, and how to combine them with inertial sensors". The PDF is at github.com/habakan/what-egocentric-embeddings-encode/blob/main/paper/what-egocentric-embeddings-encode.pdf (cell 0). The code does not cite or import any other public Kaggle notebook. It does NOT belong to the timeline/bout reconstruction family (no successor links, no adjacency). Cell 2 states: "It does not use the adjacency of the shuffled test clips". The upstream classifiers and data packing come from the author's repo via `make classifiers` and `make competition` (`src/submit/submit_band_inloop.py --lam 2 --pair --out exp028_band_inloop_pair`) [repo].
--- compute:
kernel-metadata.json: enable_gpu=true, enable_internet=false. Cell 5 sets `DEV = "cuda" if torch.cuda.is_available() else "cpu"`. All tensors are torch.float64 (`_t()` in cell 8). Each participant gets a dense n x n cosine similarity matrix (about 3k clips per test participant, so roughly 9M float64 entries, about 72 MB), plus an n x n bool mask in `knn_graph(mutual=True)`. No runtime is logged because the outputs are empty; cell 15 prints elapsed seconds, but that output was not saved. The repo README says the final system runs in 0.6 s per participant (median) on CPU. Compute-heavy cells: cell 15 (3 seeds x 3 `run_all` configs + tau grid), cell 19 (8 values of m), cell 21 (1 OOF run + 1 test run). Total memory is small, and it runs on CPU or GPU.

--- tile_construction:
The notebook receives pre-tiled data. Markdown cell 12: "The training recordings are cut like the test set: non-overlapping one-second tiles, one random valid limb per tile... All probabilities are out-of-fold (five subject-wise folds)." Limb sampling, `eval_set(seed)` (cell 13): `rng = np.random.RandomState(seed); n = len(tr['label']); sens = rng.randint(0, 4, n); idx = np.where(tr['valid'][np.arange(n), sens])[0]`. A tile whose drawn limb is invalid is DROPPED, not redrawn. Then `P = tr['P'][idx, sens]; keep = P.sum(1) > 1e-6`, which drops all-zero OOF rows. P is renormalized; U and V are taken at the same (idx, sens); F = tr['F'][idx] (per tile); y = label; sbj; rec. Seeds: cell 15 SEEDS = [42, 7, 123]; cells 19 and 21 use seed 42. The tile recipe itself is in the repo only. [repo] prepare_windows.py builds windows of 50 samples (WIN_INERTIAL = SR*WIN_SEC = 50*1) with default stride 25: `starts = np.arange(0, n - 50 + 1, 25)`, starting at sample 0 of every recording, with incomplete tails dropped. [repo] eval_pipeline.py: `tile = np.where(meta['start'] % 50 == 0)[0]`, so tiles start at samples 0, 50, 100, ... of each recording. Label is the majority vote over the 50 samples (`counts.argmax(1)`, ties go to the lower class id; NaN maps to 'null' = 0), not the center sample. valid = `np.isfinite(window).all(axis=(2,3))` per (tile, limb): any NaN/inf in the 50x3 makes it invalid; there is no flat or zero check. Limb order is SENSORS = ['right_arm','right_leg','left_leg','left_arm']. Video: frame start = start*30//50 + OFFSETS[offset], 15 frames (WIN_V=15), with OFFSETS = {'head':0,'center':7,'tail':15,'sync':8}. A repo comment says the test's 15 rows start at frame 8 ('sync'). `build()`/`load_eval_set` default to video_offset='head' (frames 0..14), and the notebook does not say which offset its dataset F uses. Exclusions and label corrections: none in the notebook or in the repo files read. There is no sbj_10-last-session, sbj_2-last-112-s or sbj_7-10-s-delay fix. Cell 22 only mentions that the paper has 'Notes on the benchmark: label errors in the training data'.
--- cv_protocol:
The OOF probabilities in train_tiles.npz come from five subject-wise folds (cell 12). [repo] cv.py `subject_folds(sbj_ids, n_splits=5, seed=SEED=42)`: unique subject ids are permuted with RandomState(42), then GroupKFold(5) groups by sbj_id, so sbj_X and sbj_X_2 always share a fold; the docstring says 4-5 subjects per fold. [repo] train_nn.py picks the best epoch per fold on that fold's validation F1 (slightly optimistic OOF), and test_pred is the mean of the fold models. Evaluation in the notebook: the 'test-like' tile set is resampled with limb-assignment seeds [42, 7, 123] (cell 15) and scores are averaged. The pipeline runs per participant (`run_all` loops over `np.unique(d['sbj'])`). The count band acts per recording (`rec`) on train and per participant on test (cell 21 sets `rec=te['sbj'].astype(str)`). The decision threshold tau is tuned on the same OOF it is scored on (`tune_tau`, grid np.linspace(0, 2.0, 41)), so the reported CV is slightly optimistic. The final tau is tuned on seed 42 only and reused for the test set (cell 20/21). There is no nested CV and the graph hyperparameters are not cross-validated in the notebook.

--- window_blend:
There is no per-window IMU weight w in this notebook; P arrives already blended ([repo] LGB 1.5 : six NNs 1 each, arithmetic mean). Fusion happens inside the graph system (cell 11, `system`). P is the anchor of label propagation. U enters only the graph geometry as 0.5*sqrt(U) concatenated to the PC-dropped, centered, L2-normalized video features. V is pooled log-linearly after every stage: `lg = (1-gi)*log(Q) + gi*log(V)`, with gi = g/(len(stages)+1) = 0.2/3 ≈ 0.0667 per stage (3 applications; total weight g = 0.2). Final decision: `prior_correct(Q, tau) = Q / Q.mean(0)**tau`, then argmax. Scores (markdown only): inertial alone 0.648; + propagation (g=0, m=0) 0.830; + video pooling (g=0.2) 0.843; + 30-PC removal 0.852 (paper system, 3 seeds); final with band + lambda=2: 0.886 CV (3 seeds), 0.894 public LB.
--- graph_and_propagation:
Everything runs per participant (`run_all`, cell 11).
(1) `graph_features(F, U, m=30)`: `drop_top_pcs(F, m)`, i.e. c = F - mean, SVD, project out the top-m right singular vectors (cell 8). Then center, L2-normalize, and concat `0.5*torch.sqrt(U)`.
(2) In `system`: Fn = G - G.mean(0), L2-normalized.
(3) `knn_graph(Fn, k=30, mutual=True)`: cosine sims, diagonal set to -inf, `k = min(k, n-2)`, topk. Non-mutual edges get weight -1e9.
(4) `propagate(P, nn, w, alpha=0.75, iters=5, sharp=10.0)`: neighbour weights ww = softmax(10*cos) over the k neighbours, then repeat 5 times `Q = (1-alpha)*P + alpha*sum(ww*Q[nn])`.
(5) `sharpen(Q, temp=1.4)`: Q**1.4, renormalized.
(6) `gate_blend` with V (gi = g/3).
(7) `apply_band` (beta).
(8) For each lam in stages: rebuild kNN from `torch.cat([Fn, lam*torch.sqrt(Q)], 1)` and re-propagate. The anchor is now the current Q, not the original P. Then sharpen, gate_blend and band again.
Final config FINAL = dict(stages=(2.0, 2.0), beta=0.5, pair=(0.45, 0.58, 0.5)) with defaults g=0.2, k=30, alpha=0.75, iters=5, temp=1.4, m=30, so 1 initial stage + 2 refinement stages. The paper system uses stages=(4.0, 4.0) and beta=0.
PC-removal sweep (cell 19, seed 42): MS = [0, 5, 10, 20, 30, 50, 80, 120]. The sweep outputs are not logged. Markdown cell 16 says neighbour purity is 0.73 at m=0 and 0.63 at m=30, and that F1 rises up to about m=30. Purity is measured with non-mutual k=30 on PC-dropped, centered F.
--- links:
None. The notebook is deliberately leak-free. Cell 2: "It does **not** use the adjacency of the shuffled test clips." There are no successor or link prediction, no ordering features and no bout reconstruction. Cell 22 mentions the paper's appendix discusses 'the adjacency of shuffled clips'. [repo] sec8_protocol/probe_chain*.py and probe_succ_all.py exist but are not part of this notebook.
--- counts_constraint:
Markdown cell 9 describes it as a 'protocol constraint', used only in the final submission. Cell 10 constants: LO, HI = 78, 126 clips per activity per recording (the 5–95% range in training), and PAIRS = [(11,12), (13,14), (16,17)] as (plain, complex), i.e. push-ups, sit-ups, lunges.
`band_bias(L, lo=78, hi=126, step=0.1, iters=200)`: on log-probs L, repeat up to 200 times. Count argmax per class; for classes 1..18 (null excluded) add +0.1 if count < lo or -0.1 if count > hi; stop once all are inside.
`pair_bias(L, lo, hi, step=0.05, iters=200)`: for each pair, shift d by 0.05 until the complex share nc/(na+nc) is in [lo, hi]. It sets b[plain] = -d and b[complex] = +d, and breaks if na+nc == 0.
`apply_band(Q, rec, beta, pair)`: per recording, B = beta*band_bias(L), then B += pair[2]*pair_bias(L+B, pair[0], pair[1]); returns Q*exp(B) renormalized. Only a fraction beta of the fitted bias is applied, so counts are nudged rather than forced.
Final: beta=0.5, pair=(0.45, 0.58, 0.5), i.e. complex share 45–58% with half the pair bias. It is applied after every stage (3 times) inside `system`. Null takes the remainder. On test, rec = participant (one recording each). No test labels are used; it assumes the protocol holds. Cell 9 says the complex variant takes 45–58% of each pair in training.
--- refiner:
There is no boundary or temporal refiner (there is no temporal order to refine). The final stage is the decision rule `prior_correct(Qt, tau).argmax(1)` (cell 21), i.e. divide by the mean predicted prior raised to tau. tau comes from `tune_tau(run_all(d, **FINAL), d['y'])` on seed-42 OOF tiles, with grid np.linspace(0, 2.0, 41). Cell 21 prints the NULL share and the min/max clip count per class (outputs not saved).

--- outputs_written:

--- plug_in_point:
The only per-window probability entry points are `tr['P']`/`te['P']`, and optionally `tr['U']`/`te['U']` and `tr['V']`/`te['V']`, right after cell 5 loads `tr = dict(np.load(DATA/'train_tiles.npz'))` and `te = dict(np.load(DATA/'test.npz'))`. Add a new cell between cell 5 and cell 8 that overwrites or blends these dict entries; nothing downstream needs to change.

TEST: te['P'] must be (12234,19), float, rows aligned to te['id'] (submission is written positionally with te['id']). Our test probs are in test-id order, so reindex: `pos = pd.Index(our_ids).get_indexer(te['id']); assert (pos >= 0).all(); Pe = ours[pos]; Pe = Pe / Pe.sum(1, keepdims=True)`. Then replace (`te['P'] = Pe`) or blend: arithmetic `te['P'] = (1-w)*te['P'] + w*Pe`, or geometric with renormalization. te['P'] is never renormalized in cell 21, so normalize it yourself.

TRAIN OOF: tr['P'] must be (n_tiles, 4, 19), indexed [tile, limb], with zero rows where a limb is invalid. eval_set (cell 13) drops rows with P.sum <= 1e-6 and renormalizes. To map our stride-25 OOF (n_windows, 4, 19) onto tiles, first print `tr.keys()`. If a 'start' key exists, select our windows with (rec, start) equal and start % 50 == 0. If not, rebuild per rec: [repo] tiles are the stride-25 windows with start % 50 == 0, starting at sample 0 of each recording, probably in recording order then start order. Verify the alignment by checking that tr['label'] equals our majority label at those starts (the repo uses majority over 50 samples) and that tr['valid'] matches our per-limb validity (all-finite over 50x3). Permute our limb axis to the repo order ['right_arm','right_leg','left_leg','left_arm']; confirm it via the valid pattern. Set rows to 0 where tr['valid'] is False. Blend the same way as on test: `tr['P'] = (1-w)*tr['P'] + w*ours_tiles`.

U and V have the same layouts (tr: (n_tiles,4,19); te: (12234,19)) and can be swapped or blended the same way. U only shapes the graph (0.5*sqrt(U)); V is pooled with total weight g=0.2.

Is train OOF required? Yes, for two things. (a) tau: cell 21 tunes tau on seed-42 OOF via `tune_tau(run_all(d, **FINAL), d['y'])` and applies it to the test Qt. tau depends on P's calibration, so it must be re-tuned whenever P changes. (b) Choosing the blend weight w and re-tuning g/temp/k/alpha/m/lam. Not needed for: the count band (LO/HI = 78/126 and pair ranges 0.45–0.58 are hard-coded constants, not learned from OOF); there is no refiner training and no counts learning. A test-only swap with the old tau would run, but tau would be mis-calibrated.

Fold caveat: their OOF is 5 subject-wise folds. Ours uses scene-cluster folds. That is acceptable only if those are subject-disjoint (and sbj_X / sbj_X_2 kept together); otherwise OOF P is over-confident and tau/w are biased. Also keep the dataset attached: F (video features per tile and per test row), sbj, rec, label and valid come only from it.

--- bugs_or_risks:
[
"Nothing in the notebook has logged outputs. All scores (0.648/0.830/0.843/0.852/0.886/0.894) are markdown claims and could not be checked against printed results.",
"tau is tuned on the same OOF tiles that produce the reported CV (`tune_tau` in cells 15, 19, 21), so the CV is slightly optimistic. The final tau uses seed 42 only.",
"Mutual-kNN quirk (cells 8/11): a node with no mutual neighbour has all k weights = -1e9, so `w - w.max()` = 0 and it gets UNIFORM weights over its k non-mutual neighbours instead of being isolated.",
"Count band is absolute (78\u2013126 clips per class per recording). In CV the band acts per `rec`, but eval_set drops tiles whose random limb is invalid (no redraw), which shrinks counts. Training participants with two recordings (sbj_X, sbj_X_2) are banded per recording, which may push classes toward 78+ in a recording that lacks them. On test it acts per participant, assuming the protocol holds for subjects 22\u201325.",
"Test rows are aligned purely by position with te['id']; any external probabilities must be reindexed to te['id'] order.",
"[repo] Train F is built with video_offset='head' by default (frames 0..14 of the second), while test frames are 8..22 (the repo's own comment calls this 'sync', offset 8). It is unknown which offset the Kaggle dataset used. This only affects CV/tau tuning, not the test graph.",
"[repo] OOF P comes from per-fold models with best epoch chosen on the fold's validation F1, while test P is the average of the 5 fold models. The calibration mismatch can shift tau and the band behaviour.",
"[repo] Window label is the majority over 50 samples with no label corrections (no sbj_10 / sbj_2 / sbj_7 fixes), so CV includes known label noise.",
"Each later stage anchors propagation to the current Q, not the original P, so errors can compound across stages."
]

--- lineage:
Briano (woominyo) "LB 0.890 | WEAR@HASCA 2026 Timeline + Graph" (Apache 2.0) supplies the whole base pipeline: test-like tiles and folds, window nets, L0/L2 link scorers with ridge boundary predictors and 65 pair features, tabular experts T and S3, graph propagation with self-training, and Sinkhorn count calibration. Briano's L0 links are adapted from Hanh Tran (honghanhhh) "WEAR@HASCA LB 0.9". Jiwei Liu "public fast GPU inference" (LB 0.9077) was the reproduction target. goodpjw2008 "WEAR@HASCA | Learned Links + Counts | LB 0.92942" adds the LOCAL label check (off), the two-tower matcher, the self-supervised ridge, the L3 scorer, link bagging, kNN smoothing g=1.5, the learned count prior, pseudo-label adapted T, and the boundary refiner. This notebook (sujanmajhisuzan) is that notebook with only cell 0 and cell 38 changed: 5 refiner seeds, a threshold grid, and temporal_island_filter.
--- compute:
Kaggle GPU T4 x2 is required (cell 5: "select the accelerator GPU T4 x2 ... Expect about two hours in total"). The 6 window models train on both GPUs in parallel threads, about 30 min (cell 5). The two-tower matcher trains 2 seeds, one per GPU, 3000 steps each. Everything else runs on CPU, with 4 BLAS threads forced in cell 6; the Gram sums in cell 23 and the tower/SSL features in cell 28 use the GPU when available. Wall-clock cap WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN=150 (env WEAR_WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN): later window runs are skipped once the cap is reached, but final_s0 and imu_s0 (ESSENTIAL) always run. Scratch is about 6.5 GB at ROOT=/tmp/wear when /tmp has more than 12 GB free, otherwise /kaggle/working/_wear (override with WEAR_ROOT); it is deleted at the end when CLEANUP is on (the default on Kaggle). Each window-training process puts all train video on the GPU, about 3.2 GB in float16 ("stagger the 3.2 GB data loads"). The parent's reference run used one NVIDIA GB10. The notebook logs RSS and peak memory per stage, but no outputs were saved, so there is no runtime or memory evidence.

--- tile_construction:
Main evaluation tiles 'sim' (cell 10, oof_windows, saved to ROOT/sim_meta.npz, sim_acc.npy, sim_vid.npy):
1. For each recording i (sorted glob order), take starts = np.arange(0, T-50+1, STRIDE=5) in 50 Hz samples. Keep only starts with starts*3//5 + 8 + 15 <= F.
2. Label = majority class within the 50 samples: cnt.argmax(1) over per-sample label counts, so ties go to the lower class index.
3. W_ok (n,4) = limbs with no NaN sample in the window. Drop windows with no valid limb.
4. Fold assignment: greedy by subject. Subjects are sorted by descending stride-5 window count and each goes to the currently least-loaded of FOLDS=5 folds.
5. For each fold 0..4: idx = that fold's windows with start % 50 == 0, i.e. non-overlapping 1-s tiles at phase 0 of each recording. rng = np.random.default_rng(1234+fold); cp = cumsum(W_ok/W_ok.sum); sensor = (rng.random(len(idx))[:,None] > cp).sum(1), a uniform random valid limb.
6. Row order: fold 0..4, then recording index, then start.
7. IMU = acc[start:start+50, sensor] (N,50,3) in g. Video frames = start*3//5 + 8 + arange(15), so for second k that is frames 30k+8 .. 30k+22.

Asserts: 69,326 rows (67,795 if cleaned); fold sizes [14353, 12995, 14625, 12768, 14585]; sha1(sensor)[:12] == 'f81d90085315'; sha1(stack[rec,start])[:12] == 'e4177f2589d7'. train_window.py's val_set uses the same idx order and default_rng(1234+fold), so its OOF rows match sim row for row (cell 13 asserts y and grp equal).

Second tile set, used only to train the L0 scorer (cell 18, train_tiles): phase-0 starts with stride 50; rng = default_rng(42 + i*17) per recording; limbs = min((rng.random > cp).sum(1), 3).

Label corrections (LOCAL, all OFF; enabled only with WEAR_CLEAN=1):
- UNLABELLED_TAIL = {"sbj_10": 2566, "sbj_2": 3452}: the recording is truncated after that second.
- LABEL_LAG = {"sbj_7": (1368, 10)}: labels after second 1368 are moved 10 s earlier.

TRAIN_SETS = {0: 2, 14: 2}: subjects 0 and 14 have two recordings (sbj_0_2, sbj_14_2). Test subjects are treated as 1 set each.
--- cv_protocol:
5 folds by subject (22 train subjects, 24 recordings), assigned greedily by window count (cells 10 and 11, identical code). The subject-to-fold map is printed in W2, but no output was saved.

The protocol is nested leave-one-fold-out: every model that feeds a later stage is fitted without the fold it is applied to. That covers the window nets (per fold), L0 scorer, S3/T (TAB_OOF), ridge boundary predictors (RIDGES[g] fitted on the other folds' sufficient statistics), L2/L3 link scorers (oof_links), two-tower (one model per fold), count regressor (fit_counts on the other folds' (subject, class) rows), adapted T (training rows from other folds plus pseudo-labels of a cross-fitted half of fold k), and refiner (Fr = fold of tile).

Not nested:
- TEST_CFGS "ranked on all 5 folds".
- QN_REF, a hard-coded 1001-point quantile table from the parent's pooled OOF L2 scores.
- RIDGE_LAM=0.1 picked by CV.
- The fork's refiner threshold, chosen on the same OOF it reports.

Test-time models are refitted on all OOF rows.

Seeds:
- Window nets: torch/np seed = seed*100+fold; fusion seeds 0–3, IMU seeds 0–1; WEAR_SEED adds 10*offset.
- Two-tower: seeds 0 and 1 (+WEAR_SEED).
- L0 scorer: 42.
- Scorer-row negatives: rng 7000+sbj.
- LK_PARAMS seed 0, with L3 seeds 0, 1, 2.
- Gumbel noise: 1000*k+sbj.
- adapt_T halves: seed k (OOF) and 99 (test).
- Refiner seeds: [42, 101, 777, 1337, 2026].

GPU training is not bit-reproducible.

--- window_blend:
Cell 13 (W4 blend):
- load_runs averages runs as mean_logp = log(mean(exp(logp))). Test logps are renormalised per run first.
- blend(fus, imu, wi=CFG['imu_w']=0.3) = log_softmax(fus + 0.3*imu), a log-linear product of experts.
- d['logp'] is the OOF blend (69326,19); t['logp'] is the test blend (12234,19). The un-blended fusion_logp and imu_logp are kept for S3.
- Window-level OOF: this version 0.7239 (scoreboard), public notebook 0.7253; code comment "(ours with 4F+2I: 0.7194 / 0.5995 / 0.7253)".

Expert blend 'B' (cell 22, tab_blend), with TAB_TEST_CHOICE = (('S3',0.5),('T',0.3)):
- B = log_softmax(0.2*window_logp + 0.5*S3_logp + 0.3*T_logp).
- OOF_LOGP_B uses the nested TAB_OOF; TEST_LOGP_B uses the full-fit models.
- Scoreboard: '+ tabular experts S3 and T' 0.8776 after label propagation, L0 links and calibration (public notebook 0.877).

Stage B (cell 36): B2 = log_softmax(0.2*d['logp'] + 0.5*S3 + 0.3*TA), the same formula with adapted T.
--- graph_and_propagation:
Diagnostic only (cells 8/19, context_baseline): label_prop(k=10, tau=0.1, use_p=0.5, alpha=0.9, iters=10), then link_smooth(alpha 0.5, 10 iters).

PLP (cell 20): PLP_OOF and PLP_TEST = label_prop of exp(logp) with CFG['lp']=(10,0.1,0.5,0.9). They are used only as link pair features (bc_lp, maxp_i, maxp_j).

Main graph (cell 26; Runner, label_prop_generic, graph_P):
- Per subject, SubjectKNN keeps the top K=40 neighbours by similarity S = cosine(emb) + use_p*<sqrt p_i, sqrt p_j> (use_p 0.5; computed on P0, or Qsrc=P0 in self-training).
- knn_W keeps k=10 with weights exp((s - max)/tau), tau=0.1 (0.3 if WEAR_WHITEN>0), symmetrised by max. norm='row'.
- The link graph Lw = link_W(succ, score, b): weight sigmoid((score - b)/1), symmetric. score is the quantile-normalised (qnorm, QN_REF) link log-odds.
- Iteration: g = A_knn@f; mixed with links g = (1 - bb)*g + bb*(rownorm(Lw)@f), where bb = beta*min(deg/2, 1) for 'abs' mode; then f = alpha*g + (1 - alpha)*f0, 20 iterations.
- Self-training rounds: Q = calibrate(norm(F^T)) with T=2.0, then re-propagate from norm(P0^(1-w) * Q^w) with Qsrc=P0.
- Before the kNN graph is built, the embedding is smoothed: E + g*rownorm(Lw)@E, one hop. Public g=0.5; this notebook g=1.5 (OUR_CFGS).
- Configs, written g/mode beta/b/alpha/rounds w. TEST_CFGS: g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r3w0.5, g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5, g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r2w0.7, g0.5/abs0.6/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5, g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r2w0.7. OUR_CFGS are the same with g1.5 (plus '/t0.3' when whitened). graph_P takes the arithmetic mean of the 5 configs.
- emb = window-net video embedding from final_s0 (KNN_EMB_OOF/TEST). Optional whitening with WEAR_WHITEN=0.5 (OFF): per-subject centring, PCA on OOF[::3], divide by var^0.5.

Link bagging (cell 32): BAG_K=8 matchings. Member 0 is the plain Hungarian matching; 7 more add Gumbel noise (BAG_TAU=0.3) to the candidate log-odds, then re-run lk_assign and qnorm the clean log-odds of the chosen link. bag_P decodes each matching and takes the geometric mean of the 8 graph_P outputs. A perturbed matching keeps 84% of the plain links.
--- links:
L0 (cell 18):
- Candidates per subject: union of the top-40 tail-head 3-frame cosine, top-15 last-first frame, and top-15 mean-pooled.
- 12 pair features: fwd, frame, pooled, rev, row/column dominance, margin, log row/column ranks, same-limb flag, IMU gap, extrapolation gap.
- LGBMClassifier: 200 rounds, lr 0.05, 31 leaves, min_child 40, class_weight balanced, 4 negatives per tile. Trained on train_tiles of the other folds' recordings (OOF) or on all recordings (test).
- Hungarian linear_sum_assignment on log-odds; links below -1.0 dropped; every cycle broken at its weakest link.
- Reported: linked 92.5%, exact successor 32.9%, same bout 88.7% (comment: 0.924 / 0.330 / 0.887). Saved to links_L0.npz. Used by S3's neighbor_block and the diagnostic.

L2, public combined links (cells 23-25):
- Ridge boundary predictors from label-free sufficient statistics of consecutive tile pairs at stride STRIDE_B=10. Video blocks [First, Head3, Tail3, Last, Mean] (5x768+1) predict the next tile's head / previous tile's tail, RIDGE_LAM 0.1, SHRINK 0.1. Per-limb-pair IMU Gaussian ridge, IMU_LAM 1e-3. r2 about 0.58.
- Candidates come from 11 SOURCES (th r40, lf r15, mm r15, lfc r15, imu r8/c5, extlin r25/c10, lfz c30, ext2 r25, dual r25), KCAP 160, plus the ridge-score top KNEW=20 per model.
- 65 features: BASE49 (stored as float16), which include window-prob Bhattacharyya bc_w, PLP bc_lp, maxp_i and maxp_j; plus NEW16 ridge features.
- LightGBM: objective cross_entropy, lr 0.05, 63 leaves, min_data 100, 300 rounds. Rows = the true successor plus LK_NEG=16 random negatives per tile. Comment: 1,173,696 rows / 0.0549 positive / 0.930 candidate recall.
- Test log-odds are averaged over the 5 fold ridge variants, then lk_assign (floor -50) and qnorm.
- Reported L2: linked 94.8%, exact 56.2%, same bout 93.7%, pipeline 0.9040; test linked about 0.951.

L3 (cells 28-30), this pipeline's links:
- 88 features: the 65 above + two-tower 2 seeds x 8 (row/column log-softmax both directions, dominance, log ranks; a training subject scored by its held-out fold model, a test subject by the mean of the 5 fold models) + 7 self-supervised features (ssl_scores: per-subject ridge with lam 0.01 mapping the first frame to the last frame and back, applied across tiles).
- LK_SEEDS=3 scorers with averaged log-odds. OOF from leave-one-fold-out scorers; test from all-subject scorers, averaged over 5 ridge variants.
- Reported: linked 96.0%, exact successor 66.0%, same bout 94.5%, pipeline 0.9138. Gain share: public features 26%, two-tower 67%, self-supervised 7%.
- What-if pipelines: true next tile 0.9293; random tile of the same bout 0.9582.
--- counts_constraint:
Fixed prior (cells 8/26):
- finish() sharpens P^(1/sharpen_T=0.5) = P^2, then calibrate() runs Sinkhorn per subject, 50 iterations.
- Each exercise column targets per_ex=97 * sets; null = max(n - 18*97*sets, null_min 0.05*n); then argmax.
- sets = TRAIN_SETS {0:2, 14:2} for OOF and {} (1 each) for test.
- The same calibration runs inside self-training (Runner.cal, with T=2.0).

Learned prior (cell 34, decode, passes=2):
- For each (subject, class 1..18), profile_features on mats [P (bagged graph output), exp(B)]: the sorted margin log q_c - log max_other, read at ranks RK=[60,70,80,90,97,105,115,125,140]*sets; plus q.sum/ns, argmax count/ns, and counts of margin > 0, > -1, > 1 (each /ns); plus n/ns. That is 29 features.
- RidgeCV with alphas [1,3,10,30,100,300,1000], fitted only on rows with 55 < true < 150; predictions clipped to [70,135]. Nested by fold for OOF; fitted on all rows for test.
- count_targets: null = max(n - sum(per*sets), 0.05n), exercises rescaled to n - null.
- Pass 1 is followed by re-running bag_P with the targets inside self-training (calibrate_targets), then the counts are re-estimated once.
- Final Q = finish_targets: P^2, then Sinkhorn to the targets.
- Reported: average count error 9.8 tiles fixed vs 7.3 learned; OOF 0.917 fixed vs 0.923 learned vs 0.936 with true counts (scoreboard what-if 0.9359); shifting all counts by 3 costs 0.002.
--- refiner:
Cell 38, O7. This is the only changed code.

Inputs:
- tile_scalars: ener = log std(|acc|) + 0.01, post = mean acc, vmot = mean frame-difference norm, vmean = frame mean.
- fin = QB.argmax, the stage-B calibrated labels.

Row generation (refiner_rows), for each of the 8 matchings L3_OOF_LINKS / L3_TEST_LINKS and each chain position where fin[i] != fin[succ[i]]:
- Tiles at offsets -2..3 (REF_H=3): i, its predecessor and its predecessor's predecessor for off <= 0; j, succ(j) and succ(succ(j)) for off > 0.
- Candidate 'other' label = the other side's label; rows with cur == other are skipped.
- 37 features: 13 for the tile (offset, cur/other-is-null flags, B, log P, window logp lw and log Q differences cur-vs-other, B[null], logP[null], energy, video motion, frame-mean distance to the predecessor and successor) + 6 for each chain neighbour at -2, -1, +1, +2 (B and P differences, energy delta, same-limb flag, posture distance, same-label flag).
- Target = (y == other) and (y != fin).

Model:
- LightGBM binary with REF_PARAMS (lr 0.05, 31 leaves, min_data 50, bagging 0.8, l2 1, 300 rounds).
- 5 seeds [42, 101, 777, 1337, 2026], feature_fraction = min(0.85, 0.75 + (idx%3)*0.05), i.e. 0.75/0.80/0.85/0.75/0.80.
- OOF: nested by the tile's fold; probabilities averaged over seeds. Test: 5 full-data models, averaged.

Flip rule (refiner_flips): a tile flips to the other label whose mean probability over K=8 matchings (a missing matching counts as 0) exceeds thr, taking the best label.
- thr is chosen on OOF macro-F1 from [0.38, 0.42, 0.45, 0.48, 0.50, 0.52, 0.55, 0.58, 0.62].
- The base is no flip; best_thr starts at 0.5 and is applied to test even if no threshold beats the base.

temporal_island_filter, then applied:
- Uses only the plain matching (links[0][0]).
- If a tile's predecessor and successor share a non-null label different from the tile's, the tile is relabelled to it.
- On OOF it is kept only if the score does not drop; on test it is ALWAYS applied.

Parent's numbers (single refiner, thr 0.5): 0.9271 -> 0.9300 OOF; changes 1,215 OOF tiles and 137 test tiles. With the true order the refiner would gain +0.018.

--- plug_in_point:
Array layout the notebook expects:
- OOF: (69326,19) natural-log probabilities, rows normalised, in 'sim' order: fold 0..4, then recording index (sorted glob of train/inertial_feat/*.csv, i.e. recs[i]['stem']), then start.
- Test: (12234,19) log-probabilities in test_meta_data.csv row order, which is np.load(PROC/'test_id.npy', allow_pickle=True). Our arrays are in test-id order, so reorder explicitly, e.g. pd.DataFrame(ext, index=our_ids).loc[ids].to_numpy(). Do not assume the orders match.

Aligning our OOF (n_windows, 4, 19) on stride-25 starts:
- Inside cell 13, after `t = {...}` (recs, sim and SENSORS are in scope):
  - stems = [r['stem'] for r in recs]
  - k = [pos[(stems[r], s)] for r, s in zip(sim['rec'], sim['start'])], where pos maps (stem, start in samples) to our window row. Join on (stem, start); every sim start is a multiple of 50, so these are the even windows of our grid.
  - limb = OUR_LIMB_ORDER.index(SENSORS[sim['sensor'][r]]), with SENSORS = ['right_arm','right_leg','left_leg','left_arm']
  - P_ext_oof = ext[k, limb]
- Check coverage first: all 69,326 keys must exist.
- Sim labels are majority over the 50 samples. Windows are kept only if start*3//5 + 23 <= F and at least one limb is valid.

Option A, recommended (one insertion, everything downstream picks it up): at the end of cell 13, set
  d['logp'] = blend_log((1-w)*d['logp'] + w*np.log(np.clip(P_ext_oof, 1e-8, 1)))
and do the same for t['logp'] with P_ext_test. blend_log means subtract the row max and apply log-softmax, as in blend().
- Optionally also put our probabilities into d/t['fusion_logp'] or d/t['imu_logp']; S3 uses them as two 19-wide feature slots, so the cell-22 assert X.shape[1] == 533/361 still holds. Update the assert if you append a new block instead.
- Downstream consumers of d['logp'] / t['logp']:
  - cell 19 diagnostic
  - cell 20 PLP_OOF/PLP_TEST
  - cell 22 S3 features (logp, neighbor_block ctx, knn_block), plus 0.2*logp in blend B
  - cells 24/25 link pair features bc_w, bc_lp, maxp (they feed the L2 scorer and, through the saved pairs_*.npz, L3)
  - cell 36 B2 = 0.2*d['logp'] + 0.5*S3 + 0.3*TA
  - cell 38 refiner feature lw
- Cheap ways to tune w on OOF before the long stages:
  - window level: the print in cell 13
  - 'blend B nested OOF ... + label propagation + L0 link smoothing + calibration' in cell 22, a few minutes
  - 'public pipeline, nested OOF' in cell 34, needs ABLATION=1

Option B (keep links and S3 untouched): add our expert as a third term of the blends.
- Cell 22: OOF_LOGP_B / TEST_LOGP_B
- Cell 36: B2_OOF / B2_TEST
- Use a custom log-linear mix, e.g. a*window + 0.5*S3 + 0.3*T + w_ext*ext followed by log-softmax. tab_blend's window weight is 1 - sum(w), which goes negative if w_ext > 0.2.
- Change both cells, or stage B silently drops the expert.

Option C (skip GPU window training): pre-populate ROOT/runs/final_s0 and ROOT/runs/imu_s0. Set WEAR_ROOT to a persistent dir; run_complete() needs oof_raw.npz, test_logp_raw.npy and test_emb.npy.
- oof_raw.npz needs logp (69326,19) in sim order, y == sim['y'] and grp == sim['sbj'] (asserted in cell 13), and emb (69326,768) f16. emb is the window mean of subject-normalised video: VM/VS per train subject from all of that subject's recording frames [::3], per test subject from its own test tiles.
- Set CFG['imu_w']=0, or give an IMU-only run. Cell 12 asserts FUSION[0].name == 'final_s0' and a non-empty IMU list.
- Remove the other FULL_RUNS names or they will still be trained.

Is OOF on train tiles required? YES, for every learned stage after the window blend:
- S3 and T stacking (cell 22, trained on OOF rows)
- L2/L3 link scorers (features from logp/PLP on OOF rows)
- count regressor (profile features from the OOF graph output and exp(B))
- pseudo-label adaptation (QA_OOF labels)
- refiner and its threshold search (OOF rows, features B2/P/Q/lw)
- all reported OOF scores

Test-only substitution would put all of these stages out of distribution. Our OOF uses scene-cluster folds. If a training subject's other scenes were in the training folds, our OOF is more confident than our test predictions, and S3, the count regressor and the refiner will over-trust it. Ideally recompute our OOF with this notebook's subject folds (fold_of printed in W2), or at least compare our OOF macro-F1 on the 69,326 sim tiles with our test-level behaviour before trusting the downstream weights.

NOTEBOOK: goodpjw2008/wear-hasca-learned-links-counts-lb-0-92942, "WEAR@HASCA | Learned Links + Counts | LB 0.92942" (Kaggle id_no 136775810). Local text: /Users/randy/Desktop/Kaggle/wear/ref2/wear-hasca-learne
LB: The title claims public LB 0.92942 with nested OOF macro-F1 0.9300. That LB comes from the author's own-machine run (version 3, one NVIDIA GB10; the six window models and the first two-tower seed were reused from an earlier run). This file is version 4: the same code re-run from scratch on Kaggle T4
--- lineage:
Built on Briano's "LB 0.890 | WEAR@HASCA 2026 Timeline + Graph" (woominyo/lb-0-890-wear-hasca-2026-timeline-graph, Apache 2.0). Sections 2 and 4 (cells 7-13 and 17-26) are Briano's code, unchanged except for lines marked LOCAL. Briano's L0 links are adapted from Hanh Tran (honghanhhh/wear-hasca-lb-0-9): video-boundary candidates, LightGBM pair scorer, Hungarian assignment with cycle breaking. Reproduction target: Jiwei Liu's packaging (jiweiliu/public-fast-gpu-inference, LB 0.9077); this notebook retrains everything instead of loading saved models. Author's own additions are sections 3 and 5-9: two-tower matcher, self-supervised ridge boundary predictor, L3 scorer, link bagging, kNN g=1.5, learned count prior, pseudo-label adapted T, boundary refiner, LOFO extensions. Notebooks read for ideas but no code used: Akhyar Ahmad (Viterbi chain decoding with count bands), Avik Das (window counts per test subject), plus Evelyn_Yang_02, Nomannic, SibamSamanta07, Swathi H A, Lakhindar Pal. Part 2 by the same author (LB 0.92990) changes only the link scorer.
--- compute:
The file has no runtime logs; the only compute evidence is markdown and code. Markdown (cell 5) says to select GPU T4 x2. The window models train on both GPUs in about 30 minutes, the two-tower matcher trains two seeds (one per GPU), and the CPU stages take the rest: "about two hours in total". Cell 6 sets OMP/OPENBLAS/MKL threads to 4 ("Kaggle has 4 vCPUs"). WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN=150 (env WEAR_WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN) caps the GPU window stage; only the essential runs final_s0 and imu_s0 are guaranteed. Scratch ROOT is /tmp/wear when /tmp has more than 12e9 bytes free, else WORK/_wear ("about 6.5 GB of scratch"). Each training subprocess loads about 3.2 GB of data (30 s stagger, cell 12). A GPU check runs in a subprocess. Without a usable GPU, CPU_RUNS fall back to 4 epochs x 100 steps (the score is "far below 0.903") and the two-tower uses 300 steps. The Gram matrices in the ridge stage use the GPU when available ("saves about 5 min"). The stage() context manager prints minutes and RSS/peak, and cell 41 prints a stage-time table, but none of these outputs are in the file. The author's own run used one NVIDIA GB10; under bf16 on Ampere+, T4 uses fp16 with GradScaler.

--- tile_construction:
**Preprocessing** (cell 9, prep):
- acc = the 12 ACC_COLS as float32. valid = (T,4) per-limb no-NaN mask, computed BEFORE NaN filling. NaNs are then filled with pandas interpolate(limit_direction='both').fillna(0).
- labels = label.fillna('null').str.strip(), mapped to CLASSES indices 0..18 (0 = null).
- Limb index order is SENSORS = ['right_arm','right_leg','left_leg','left_arm'] (0..3).

**Train-tile recipe** (cell 10, oof_windows; train_window.py builds the identical list):
- WIN=50, VLEN=15, STRIDE=5. Candidate starts = np.arange(0, T-50+1, 5), kept only if start*3//5 + 8 + 15 <= F.
- Window label = argmax of per-class sample counts inside the 50 samples (majority; ties go to the lower class index, so null wins ties).
- W_ok[n,limb] = no invalid sample of that limb in the window. Windows with no valid limb are dropped.
- Folds: subjects sorted by descending window count, each assigned greedily to the least-loaded of FOLDS=5.
- Per fold: idx = that fold's windows with start % 50 == 0, i.e. non-overlapping 1-s tiles aligned at sample 0, stride 50.
- Limb draw: rng = np.random.default_rng(1234 + fold); cp = cumsum(W_ok/W_ok.sum(1)); sensor = (rng.random(len(idx))[:,None] > cp).sum(1). This is a uniform draw among valid limbs.
- Video frames = vid[start*3//5 + 8 + arange(15)], i.e. frames 8..22 of the second (load_inputs).
- Row order: fold 0..4, then within a fold by (rec, start).
- Saved to ROOT/sim_meta.npz (rec, start, sensor, y, sbj, fold), ROOT/sim_acc.npy (N,50,3) float32 in g, ROOT/sim_vid.npy (N,15,768) float16.
- Hard asserts when WEAR_CLEAN is off: N = 69,326; per-fold counts [14353, 12995, 14625, 12768, 14585]; sha1(sensor)[:12] = 'f81d90085315'; sha1(stack(rec,start))[:12] = 'e4177f2589d7'.
- train_window.py's val_set reuses default_rng(1234+fold), so the window OOF rows equal the sim rows. Cell 13 asserts y equality and grp == sbj; this works because the train sbj ids are 0..21, so SB.index(s) == s.

**Other tile sets:**
- L0 scorer training (cell 18, train_tiles): stride-50 tiles from sample 0, rng = default_rng(42 + rec*17), limbs = min((rng.random>cp).sum,3).
- Two-tower training (cell 15): two contiguous blocks of 256 tiles from one recording at a random phase 0..49, one random valid limb per tile, rotation/scale drawn once per (recording, limb).
- Ridge boundary stats (cell 23): consecutive tile pairs (s, s+50) at STRIDE_B=10.

**Label corrections:** all OFF by default (WEAR_CLEAN=0). With WEAR_CLEAN=1:
- UNLABELLED_TAIL = {'sbj_10': 2566, 'sbj_2': 3452}: seconds kept; the all-null last sessions (1,419 s and 112 s) are cut.
- LABEL_LAG = {'sbj_7': (1368, 10)}: labels from second 1368 move 10 s earlier and the last 10 s become null.
- N becomes 67,795.
- Author result: OOF 0.9415 on cleaned labels but public LB 0.9232-0.9269 versus 0.9257-0.9286 on released labels, so cleaning is off.

**Test:** meta row order. test_sensor maps the location strings via SENSORS. sets={}: every test subject is assumed to do the circuit once. In train, TRAIN_SETS = {0:2, 14:2}.
--- cv_protocol:
**Folds.** Five subject folds, built greedily: subjects sorted by stride-5 window count, each added to the least-loaded fold. Fold sizes in tiles: [14353, 12995, 14625, 12768, 14585] (69,326 tiles, 22 subjects). The same folds are used for every stage.

**Nesting (each model is applied only to subjects it did not train on):**
- Window nets (train_window.py): per fold, trained on all stride-5 windows of the other folds and scored on the held-out tiles. Test = mean of the 5 fold models' log-softmax.
- L0 scorer: fit per fold on the other folds' recordings; the test scorer is fit on all.
- Tabular S3/T: test models fit on all 69,326 OOF rows; OOF via leave-one-fold-out models (TAB_OOF, a LOCAL addition).
- Ridge boundary predictors: RIDGES[g] fit on the other 4 folds' tile-pair stats.
- L2/L3 link scorers: leave-one-fold-out (oof_links); test scorers fit on all rows, averaging log-odds over the 5 ridge variants and 3 scorer seeds.
- Two-tower: 5 fold models per seed; train tiles are embedded by their held-out fold model, test tiles by all 5.
- Count ridge: nested by the fold of each (subject, class) row.
- adapt_T: per held-out fold, plus random half cross-fitting inside the new subjects.
- Refiner: leave-one-fold-out.

**Not nested:**
- QN_REF, a fixed 1001-point quantile table from their pooled OOF L2 scores.
- PCA for the vpca features, fit on all OOF rows (label-free).
- TEST_CFGS chosen on all 5 folds; TAB weights; all hyperparameters.

**Seeds:**
- Window runs: torch/numpy seed = seed*100 + fold, with seeds 0-3 for fusion and 0-1 for IMU, plus 10*WEAR_SEED.
- Limb draw: 1234 + fold.
- L0: 42.
- Scorer negative sampling: default_rng(7000 + sbj).
- LK_PARAMS: seed 0 (L3 uses seeds 0, 1, 2).
- Two-tower: seeds 0 and 1 (plus WEAR_SEED), seed*100 + fold.
- Gumbel noise: 1000*k + sbj.
- adapt_T: seed = fold k for OOF, 99 for test.
- TAB_PARAMS: seed 0.

**Metric.** macro_f1 averages only over classes present in y or pred.

--- window_blend:
The blend has three levels.

1. **Window blend** (cell 13): d['logp'] = log_softmax(F + 0.3*I), where F = log mean probability of the 4 fusion seeds and I = the same for the 2 IMU-only seeds. Reference OOF 0.7253; this version 0.7239.
2. **Blend B** (cell 22, tab_blend): x = (1 - 0.5 - 0.3)*window_logp + 0.5*S3_logp + 0.3*T_logp, then log-softmax. The window logp gets weight 0.2; weights come from TAB_TEST_CHOICE = (('S3',0.5),('T',0.3)). This gives OOF_LOGP_B (from TAB_OOF) and TEST_LOGP_B (from TAB_TEST). Scoreboard: blend B + label propagation + L0 link smoothing + Sinkhorn = 0.8776 OOF, against 0.8464 for the window blend alone through the same context baseline (cell 19).
3. **Blend B2** (cell 36): the same with adapted T, B2 = log_softmax(0.2*d['logp'] + 0.5*TAB_OOF['S3'] + 0.3*TA_OOF). Test uses TAB_TEST['S3'] and TA_TEST. B2 feeds the final decode (stage B) and the refiner.

End to end: public pipeline 0.9040; with B2 and the full decode, 0.9271 before the refiner.
--- graph_and_propagation:
**Per-subject graph** (cell 26). There is no PC removal by default.

**Embedding:** emb = window mean over 15 frames of per-subject-normalised VideoMAE features (vid_emb in train_window.py; d['emb'] from final_s0 oof_raw.npz, t['emb'] from test_emb.npy). Optional WEAR_WHITEN=0.5 (OFF): subject-centred, projected on the training PCA axes, scaled by var^-0.5, with kNN tau 0.3. It gave OOF +0.003-0.004 but LB -0.0022 / +0.0032.

**Embedding smoothing:** Es = E + g * row_norm(Lw) @ E (one hop), with g = 1.5 in OUR_CFGS (g0.5 in the public TEST_CFGS).

**kNN graph:**
- SubjectKNN keeps K=40 candidates per subject, by similarity S = cos(Es) + use_p 0.5 * sqrt(P0)·sqrt(P0)^T.
- knn_W keeps the top k=10 with weights exp((s - smax)/tau), tau=0.1, symmetrised by max, then row-normalised (norm='row').

**Link graph:** Lw[i,succ] = sigmoid((qn_score - b)/1) with b = -2.0, symmetric.

**Propagation** (label_prop_generic): 20 iterations of g = A_knn f; g = (1-bb)*g + bb*(row_norm(Lw) f); f = alpha*g + (1-alpha)*f0. In link_mode 'abs', bb = beta*min(deg/2, 1).

**Self-training:** rounds-1 extra rounds. Q = Sinkhorn-calibrate(norm(F^2)), using the fixed 97 per exercise or the learned targets. New prior = norm(P0^(1-w) * Q^w). The kNN graph is still built from P0.

**Configurations:** five configs, arithmetic mean of their probabilities. TEST_CFGS = ['g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r3w0.5', 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5', 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r2w0.7', 'g0.5/abs0.6/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5', 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r2w0.7']. Name format g{emb_g}/{link_mode}{beta}/b{link bias}/a{alpha}/r{rounds}w{w}[/t{tau}]. OUR_CFGS replaces g0.5 with g1.5.

**Link bagging** (bag_P): one decode per bagged matching (8), then geometric mean of the probabilities.

**Final step:** finish / finish_targets: sharpen P^(1/0.5), Sinkhorn per subject (50 iterations), argmax.

**Other propagation used:**
- label_prop (cell 8, CFG lp = k 10, tau 0.1, use_p 0.5, alpha 0.9, 20 iterations), used for PLP_OOF/PLP_TEST (cell 20). These feed link features and the context_baseline diagnostic.
- link_smooth (alpha 0.5, 10 iterations), diagnostic only.

**Public-pipeline submission:** cell 26 decodes TEST_LOGP_B with the L2 links and TEST_CFGS, then fixed 97 calibration, and writes submission_public_pipeline.csv.
--- links:
**L0 links** (cell 18, Hanh Tran / Briano):
- Candidates: union of video-boundary top-k over tail3·head3 (40), last·first (15) and mean·mean (15).
- 12 pair features: fwd, frame, pooled, rev, dom_r, dom_c, margin, log ranks, same-limb, IMU gap, extrapolation gap.
- Scorer: LGBMClassifier, 200 rounds, lr 0.05, 31 leaves, balanced, 4 negatives.
- Assignment: Hungarian, links with log-odds below -1 dropped, cycles broken at the weakest link.
- Reference OOF: linked 0.924, exact 0.330, same-label 0.887 (scoreboard: 92.5% / 32.9% / 88.7% same bout).
- Used by S3's neighbor_block and the context baseline.

**L2 combined links** (cells 23-25, Briano):
- Per-fold ridge boundary predictors on subject-centred unit-frame blocks [First, Head3, Tail3, Last, Mean] (5x768+1). Forward predicts the head of j; backward predicts the tail of i. RIDGE_LAM 0.1, SHRINK 0.1, plus IMU ridge per limb pair with IMU_LAM 1e-3. Reference r2 about 0.58.
- Candidates: 11 SOURCES — th r40, lf r15, mm r15, lfc r15, imu r8, imu c5, extlin r25, extlin c10, lfz c30, ext2 r25, dual r25. Capped at KCAP=160 by source count, plus the ridge rs top KNEW=20 per model.
- 65 features: BASE49 (stored float16) + NEW16 ridge log-likelihood features. The base features include bc_w = Bhattacharyya(exp(window logp)), and bc_lp, maxp_i, maxp_j from PLP. Window probabilities therefore feed the link scorer.
- Scorer: LightGBM cross_entropy (lr 0.05, 63 leaves, min_data 100, bagging 0.8, ff 0.8, l2 1, 300 rounds), trained on the true successor plus LK_NEG=16 random negatives per tile. Reference: 1,173,696 rows, positive rate 0.0549, candidate recall 0.930.
- Test: mean log-odds over the 5 ridge variants, lk_assign (Hungarian, cycle breaking), then qnorm per subject to QN_REF. Reference test linked 0.951.
- OOF L2: linked 94.8%, exact 56.2%, same bout 93.7%.

**L3 links (ours)** (cells 27-30):
- 88 features = 65 public + 8 per two-tower seed x 2 seeds (row/column log-softmax in both directions, row/column dominance, log row/column ranks; test averages the features over the 5 fold models) + 7 self-supervised ridge features (ssl_feats, lam 0.01).
- The ridge is fit per subject on that subject's own tiles, including test subjects: first frame -> last frame within a tile, applied as last of i -> first of j.
- Three scorer seeds (LK_SEEDS=3), log-odds averaged. OOF comes from leave-one-fold-out scorers; test from all-subject scorers, also averaged over the 5 ridge variants.
- Result: linked 96.0%, exact successor 66.0%, same bout 94.5%; pipeline 0.9138 with the public blend and fixed 97.
- Gain share: 65 public 26%, two-tower 67%, self-supervised 7%.

**Link bagging** (cells 31-32): BAG_K=8 matchings. Member 0 is the plain Hungarian; members 1-7 add BAG_TAU=0.3 * Gumbel noise to the candidate log-odds. The score handed on is the clean log-odds of the chosen link, then qnorm. A perturbed matching keeps 84% of the plain links.

**What-ifs:** true next tile → 0.9293; random tile of the same bout → 0.9582.
--- counts_constraint:
**Public calibration** (cell 8, calibrate):
- Per-subject Sinkhorn, 50 iterations.
- Each exercise column targets (n - null)/18, where null = max(n - 18*per_ex*sets, null_min*n), per_ex=97, null_min=0.05, sets = TRAIN_SETS {0:2, 14:2} for train and {} (= 1) for test.
- Applied after sharpening P^(1/sharpen_T), sharpen_T 0.5, and inside the self-training rounds with T=2.0.

**Learned count prior** (cell 34, ours):
- Features: profile_features over two matrices, the bagged graph probabilities P and exp(blend B).
- For each (subject, class c=1..18), sc = sorted log-margin log q_c - log max_{other} q, taken at ranks RK = [60,70,80,90,97,105,115,125,140] (scaled by sets), plus sum q/ns, argmax count/ns and counts of sc > 0, > -1 and > 1 (divided by ns), plus n/ns. That is 29 features.
- Model: RidgeCV(alphas=[1,3,10,30,100,300,1000]) on standardised features, fitted only on rows with 55 < true count < 150. Predictions clipped to [70,135].
- OOF counts are nested by subject fold; test counts come from a model fit on all 22 subjects.
- count_targets: per = cnt*sets; null = max(n - sum(per), 0.05*n); class targets = per*(n - null)/sum(per). These are fed to calibrate_targets.
- passes=2: the pass-1 targets go into a re-decode (Runner.cal uses them in the self-training rounds), the counts are re-estimated, and finish_targets runs.

**Results:**
- Average count error: 9.8 tiles with fixed 97 → 7.3 learned.
- OOF: fixed 97 = 0.917, learned = 0.923, true counts = 0.936 (scoreboard: 0.9359 versus 0.9232).
- Shifting all counts by 3 tiles costs 0.002. A third pass, noisy targets and LightGBM mixes did not help.

**Session structure:** only via sets; section 10 found forbidding the other exercise group flat.
--- refiner:
**Boundary refiner** (cell 38, O7). REF_H=3, REF_THR=0.5, REF_ROUNDS=300. REF_PARAMS: binary, lr 0.05, 31 leaves, min_data 50, ff 0.8, bagging 0.8/1, l2 1, 4 threads.

**Candidate tiles:** for each of the 8 bagged matchings, walk the chains. For every linked pair (i, j=succ[i]) whose final labels differ (fin = QB argmax; A = fin[i], B = fin[j]), take the tiles at offsets -2..0 before i and the tiles from j onward up to 2 more steps after it. Each such tile becomes a candidate to flip to the other side's label.

**37 features:**
- Offset, cur==null, other==null.
- Margins cur-vs-other in B2 (blend logp), log PB (graph), lw = d['logp'] (raw window blend) and log QB (calibrated).
- B[g,0], lP[g,0], movement energy log std|a|, video motion, video-mean distance to the predecessor and successor.
- For neighbours at -2, -1, +1 and +2: B and P margins, energy difference, same limb, posture distance (same limb only), and whether the neighbour's label equals cur.

**Training:** target = (y == other) & (y != fin). OOF is nested by fold; the test model is fit on all OOF rows. A tile flips if its mean flip probability over K=8 matchings exceeds 0.5 (the best other label is taken).

**Results:** OOF 0.9271 → 0.9300. Changes 1,215 OOF tiles and 137 test tiles. With the true order it would gain +0.018. The output is REF_OOF (scoring only) and the test labels in submission.csv.

--- plug_in_point:
**1. Primary hook: cell 13 ("W4 blend"), right after the dicts d and t are built and before cell 18.**
Every downstream stage reads d['logp'] (OOF) and t['logp'] (test). Both are (N,19) LOG-probabilities, log-softmax normalised, because the code calls np.exp on them everywhere. They feed:
- cell 19 context baseline;
- cell 20 PLP_OOF/PLP_TEST, which feed the bc_lp, maxp_i and maxp_j link features;
- cells 24/25 bc_w = Bhattacharyya of exp(logp), a base link feature stored in pairs_{s}.npz / testpairs_{s}.npz, so the window probabilities change the L2/L3 links;
- cell 22 S3 input columns (d['logp'] plus neighbor_block and knn_block) and the tab_blend window term (weight 0.2);
- cell 34 count profiles, through exp(B);
- cell 36 B2;
- cell 38 refiner feature lw.

Insert:
```
EXT_O = np.log(np.clip(ext_oof, 1e-7, 1))
EXT_T = np.log(np.clip(ext_test, 1e-7, 1))
for D_, E_ in ((d, EXT_O), (t, EXT_T)):
    x = (1 - a) * D_['logp'] + a * E_
    D_['logp'] = (x - np.logaddexp.reduce(x, 1, keepdims=True)).astype(np.float32)
```
a = 1 replaces the window blend outright. Optionally also put the external logp into d['fusion_logp'] / d['imu_logp']; those are used only as S3 columns, so the 533-column assert in cell 22 still holds.

**2. Required array layouts.**
- OOF: ext_oof must be (69326,19) in sim row order, i.e. the rows of ROOT/sim_meta.npz / the sim dict:
  - rows ordered by fold 0..4, then by recording index, then start;
  - rec indexes recordings.json, which follows sorted(train/inertial_feat/*.csv) stems;
  - start is in 50 Hz samples, a multiple of 50 from 0;
  - sensor: 0 = right_arm, 1 = right_leg, 2 = left_leg, 3 = left_arm.
- Mapping from the stride-25, 4-limb OOF: row r → U_stem[recs[rec[r]]['stem']][start[r]//25][limbmap[sensor[r]]]. That is every second stride-25 window, assuming the external grid starts at 0 with the same frame rule start*3//5+8..+22. Remap the external limb order to SENSORS. All 69,326 rows must be covered: assert, and fall back to the notebook's own logp for any gap.
- Test: ext_test must be (12234,19) in test_meta_data.csv row order. Reindex: `ids = np.load(PROC/'test_id.npy', allow_pickle=True); pos = {i: k for k, i in enumerate(ext_ids)}; ext_test = ext[[pos[i] for i in ids]]`. Do not assume that "test-id order" equals meta order.

**3. Secondary, lower-risk hook: a third expert in tab_blend at both places.**
- Cell 22: OOF_LOGP_B = tab_blend(d['logp'], [(TAB_OOF['S3'],0.5), (TAB_OOF['T'],0.3), (EXT_O,w)]), and the same for TEST_LOGP_B with TAB_TEST and EXT_T. Also re-save test_logp_b.npy.
- Cell 36: B2_OOF / B2_TEST with (TA_OOF / TA_TEST, 0.3) and (EXT, w).
- The window weight becomes 0.2 - w, so keep w ≤ 0.2 or lower the S3/T weights.
- This touches the decode, counts, adapted-T pseudo-labels and refiner, but not the link scorer, S3 or PLP.

**4. OOF on train tiles is mandatory for later stages.** Supplying only test probabilities would mis-specify:
- S3 and T test models (cell 22), trained on OOF rows and features;
- L2/L3 scorers, trained on OOF pair features containing bc_w/bc_lp/maxp;
- the count ridge (cell 34), fit on OOF profiles of exp(B_oof) and the graph P;
- adapt_T (cell 36), pseudo-labelled from QA_OOF;
- the refiner (cell 38), trained on OOF chains with the B2, PB and lw features;
- every printed SCORES value.

The OOF should be subject-honest, i.e. never predicted by a model that saw that subject, as with the notebook's 5 subject folds. Scene-cluster OOF where the same subject's other scenes were in training will be over-confident relative to test, and the learned components will over-trust it. In that case prefer hook 3 with a small w, or temper (multiply the logp by a factor below 1 before renormalising). Test probabilities should be the fold-ensemble average, matching how the notebook builds t['logp'].

**5. Cheap tuning before the full run.** Use the CPU-only prints:
- cell 19: window logp + label propagation + L0 + calibration;
- cell 22: "blend B nested OOF macro F1 ...; + label propagation + L0 link smoothing + calibration ...";
- then the cell 34 SCORES at stage A.

**6. Skipping GPU window training is possible.** Replace cells 12-13. You must still supply:
- d['emb'] / t['emb']: the per-subject-normalised mean of the 15 frames. Train stats come from every 3rd frame of all of the subject's recordings; test stats from that subject's test tile frames (vid_emb with --vnorm subject).
- d['fusion_logp'] and d['imu_logp'], 19 columns each, for S3.

Cell 16's two-tower still needs a GPU (300 steps on CPU fallback). The skip markers (.done_prep, .done_simwin, run_complete, emb_test_f*.npz) and WEAR_ROOT allow resuming from a writable copy of a previous ROOT.

**7. Asserts that break if the inputs change:**
- cell 10: 69326 rows, fold counts, sha1 hashes;
- cell 13: _o['y'] == sim['y'];
- cell 22: X.shape[1] == 533 / 361.
