--- bugs_or_risks:
[
"**Reproducibility.** GPU training is not bit-reproducible: the same code gave 0.0037 LB spread, and v2 scored 0.92463 on Kaggle vs 0.92718 locally. Re-running version 4 on Kaggle T4 (fp16 path) may land below 0.92942. The later stage gains (+0.001-0.004 OOF each) are within the LB noise.",
"**Nothing intermediate survives on Kaggle.** CLEANUP defaults on there and cell 41 runs rmtree(ROOT), and ROOT is /tmp/wear anyway. This run therefore does not leave the GPU artifacts (runs/*, link*/, pairs) in /kaggle/working. Part 2 consumes a separately uploaded dataset, goodpjw2008/wear-hasca-part2-gpu-outputs. To keep artifacts, set WEAR_CLEANUP=0 with WEAR_ROOT under /kaggle/working (watch the ~6.5 GB output limit) or WEAR_DUMP=1.",
"**Hard asserts.** Cell 9 checks the recording count and total lengths; cell 10 checks 69326 rows, the fold sizes and sha1 hashes of sensor and (rec,start); cell 22 checks feature counts 533/361. Any change to the tile recipe or extra expert columns fails these.",
"**Majority-label ties go to null.** argmax over class counts resolves ties to the lowest index (null).",
"**Test subjects assumed to do one circuit.** t['sets'] = {} means count targets assume each test subject did the circuit once. If a test subject repeated it, fixed-97 or learned counts (clipped at 135) would be badly wrong.",
"**Train/test feature mismatch in the link scorer.** On test, two-tower features are averaged over 5 fold models and log-odds over 5 ridge variants; OOF rows use a single fold model and ridge. QN_REF is a hard-coded quantile table from the author's run, so link-score scale is fixed regardless of the scorer.",
"**S3 trained on noisier inputs than it sees at test.** The S3 test model is trained on single-fold OOF window logps but applied to 5-fold-averaged test logps. External probabilities should mimic this.",
"**Mild OOF optimism.** Not everything is nested: TEST_CFGS, the blend weights, QN_REF, the count-prior design and refiner choices were selected on the same 22-subject OOF.",
"**What-ifs cost runtime.** ABLATION=1 (default) runs extra decodes; set WEAR_ABLATION=0 to save time.",
"**Saved probabilities are pre-refiner.** final_probabilities.npz stores QB_* (calibrated, before the refiner), while submission.csv contains the refined labels.",
"**Fold scheme mismatch for external inputs.** The external OOF probabilities use scene-cluster folds, not these 5 subject folds. Plugging them in without subject-honest OOF risks over-confident features in S3, the link scorer, the count ridge and the refiner.",
"**Stronger context experts did not help.** Section 10 found stronger context-using models hurt or did nothing (final 0.904 \u2192 0.895-0.902), and adapted window nets were flat. Only independent per-tile evidence is likely to help, so external models must not use neighbour or test-time kNN smoothing."
]

--- lineage:
Base pipeline is Briano's "LB 0.890 | WEAR@HASCA 2026 Timeline + Graph" (woominyo/lb-0-890-wear-hasca-2026-timeline-graph, Apache 2.0). It supplies the tiles and fold split, the window classifier, the L0 and L2 link scorers (ridge boundary predictors, 65 pair features), tabular experts T and S3, graph propagation with self-training, and Sinkhorn count calibration; cells 7 to 26 are Briano's code with lines marked LOCAL. Briano's L0 links are adapted from Hanh Tran (honghanhhh/wear-hasca-lb-0-9). Jiwei Liu's packaging (jiweiliu/public-fast-gpu-inference, LB 0.9077) was the reproduction target; his "private inference" notebooks were read and not used. The LambdaRank link-scorer idea comes from Prabhav Semwal (yeashusemwal/ranked-timelines-boundary-refiner-lb-0-928, LB 0.92875); none of its code is used. ItsmeSujan (sujanmajhisuzan/wear-hasca-sota-bagged-boundary-refiner, LB 0.92843) was read only. Direct predecessor: the author's Part 1 (goodpjw2008/wear-hasca-learned-links-counts-lb-0-92942). Author's own additions: two-tower boundary matcher, self-supervised ridge boundary predictor, ranked L3 scorer, link bagging, kNN smoothing g=1.5, learned count prior, cross-fitted pseudo-label adaptation of T, boundary refiner, and leave-one-fold-out versions of every stage.
--- compute:
This Kaggle version is CPU-only. The GPU stages (6 window nets, 3 two-tower seeds) are skipped because their outputs come from the attached dataset goodpjw2008/wear-hasca-part2-gpu-outputs; a stage is skipped when run_complete() or the emb_test_f*.npz files exist. Runtime claims are from markdown only, since nothing is logged: "about three hours on CPU in total" (cell 5). If trained from scratch on GPU T4 x2: window models about 30 min, the three two-tower seeds about 30 min, the ranker about 30 min more than Part 1's scorer, and 60 negatives add about 20 min (3.6x the rows). Kaggle has 4 vCPUs; OMP/OPENBLAS/MKL threads are set to 4. Scratch ROOT=/tmp/wear (about 6.5 GB; used only if /tmp has more than 12 GB free, otherwise WORK/_wear), deleted at the end when WEAR_CLEANUP=1 (the default on Kaggle). Each window-training subprocess loads 3.2 GB of data. WINDOW_BUDGET_MIN=150 caps the GPU stage. The Gram accumulation uses the GPU when present (saves about 5 min). The stage() context manager prints RSS and peak memory, but no values were saved. Bit reproducibility: the CPU stages differ between x86 and ARM (98.7% identical labels, LB 0.92620 vs 0.92990). GPU reruns differ too (Part 1: 0.92444 on Kaggle vs 0.92942 locally, 98.2% identical; two GPU seeds gave 0.92686 vs 0.92316 with 98.1% identical).

--- tile_construction:
Cell 10, oof_windows(), defines the test-like train tiles ('sim'). Steps:

- Recording index i follows the sorted order of train/inertial_feat/*.csv stems, stored in PROC/recordings.json.
- Candidate starts: np.arange(0, T-WIN+1, STRIDE) with WIN=50 and STRIDE=5, in 50 Hz samples. A start is kept only if start*3//5 + 8 + VLEN <= F, with VLEN=15.
- Video frames per tile are start*3//5 + 8 + arange(15), i.e. frames 8..22 of the second at 30 FPS.
- Label: majority. cnt = class counts over the 50 samples, y = cnt.argmax(1), so ties go to the lower class index (null wins ties).
- W_ok (n,4) marks limbs with no originally-NaN sample in the window. Windows with no valid limb are dropped (keep = W_ok.any(1)).
- Folds: subjects sorted by descending window count, each assigned greedily to the fold with the smallest current load. FOLDS=5.
- Tiles: per fold, keep idx with W_start % 50 == 0. These are non-overlapping 1 s tiles aligned to recording sample 0, with phase 0 only.
- Limb per tile: rng = np.random.default_rng(1234 + fold); cp = cumsum(W_ok/W_ok.sum); sensor = (rng.random(n)[:,None] > cp).sum(1). This draws uniformly among the valid limbs. Sensor index order is SENSORS = [right_arm, right_leg, left_leg, left_arm].
- Row order: fold 0..4 concatenated; within a fold, ascending recording index, then ascending start.
- Saved to ROOT/sim_meta.npz with keys rec, start, sensor, y, sbj, fold, plus sim_acc.npy (N,50,3) float32 and sim_vid.npy (N,15,768) float16.
- Assertions: 69,326 rows (67,795 if WEAR_CLEAN); per-fold counts [14353, 12995, 14625, 12768, 14585]; sha1(sensor)[:12]=='f81d90085315'; sha1(stack([rec,start]))[:12]=='e4177f2589d7'.
- train_window.py val_set() regenerates exactly these tiles in the same order (same rng 1234+fold, same rule), so each oof_raw.npz row matches a sim row. Cell 13 asserts _o['y']==sim['y'] and _o['grp']==sim['sbj'].

Other tilings in the notebook:
- Window-net training samples use every 0.1 s start (stride 5), the majority label, class-balanced sampling (freq^-0.5) and a random valid limb drawn by torch.multinomial.
- The L0 scorer uses its own tiles (cell 18 train_tiles): stride 50 from 0, limbs from default_rng(seed + i*17) with seed=42, clipped to <=3.
- The two-tower matcher trains on random-phase contiguous blocks.

Label corrections are OFF by default. With WEAR_CLEAN=1 (cell 6/9):
- UNLABELLED_TAIL = {'sbj_10': 2566, 'sbj_2': 3452} (seconds kept; sbj_10 loses 1,419 s and sbj_2 loses 112 s).
- LABEL_LAG = {'sbj_7': (1368, 10)}: labels after second 1368 move 10 s earlier, and the last 10 s become null.
- File naming: the paper's sbj_18* and sbj_19† are sbj_0_2 and sbj_14_2 here. TRAIN_SETS = {0: 2, 14: 2}, since subjects 0 and 14 recorded the circuit twice.
--- cv_protocol:
There are 5 subject folds, assigned greedily by window count (cell 10, the same algorithm as in train_window.py); the subject-to-fold map is fold_of. All 22 train subjects (0..21) are scored. Every model feeding a later stage is fitted leave-one-fold-out ("nested OOF"):
- Window nets: 5 fold models per run. Test predictions are the mean of the 5 fold models' log-softmax.
- Two-tower matcher: per fold. Test features are averaged over the 5 fold models.
- Tabular S3 and T: LOFO for OOF; the test models are trained on all OOF rows.
- Ridge boundary predictors RIDGES[g]: fit on folds != g. Test uses all 5.
- L0, L2 and L3 scorers: L0 is per fold on raw train_tiles. L2 has a LOFO version only under ABLATION; its test model LINK_MODEL is trained on all rows. L3 uses 5 LOFO scorers x 3 seeds, and the test scorers are trained on all 22 subjects.
- Count-prior ridge: nested by fold. Test uses a fit on all rows.
- Pseudo-label adaptation of T: per fold, with cross-fitted halves.
- Refiner: LOFO. The test model is trained on all rows.

Not nested (selected on full OOF, so slightly optimistic): the fixed graph configs TEST_CFGS ("ranked on all 5 folds"), the QN_REF quantile table (pooled OOF combined-link scores), and all hand-set constants.

Seeds:
- Window nets: --seed 0..3 (fusion) and 0..1 (IMU); torch and numpy seed = seed*100 + fold; the validation rng is 1234+fold.
- Two-tower: seeds 0, 1, 2 (+WEAR_SEED).
- L0 scorer: seed 42.
- L2/L3 negatives: default_rng(7000+sbj).
- L3 LightGBM seeds 0..2; Gumbel bagging seeds 1000*k+sbj.
- adapt_T halves: seed k (fold) for OOF, 99 for test. TAB_PARAMS seed 0.

Metric: macro_f1 (cell 8) averages F1 over classes present in y or pred.
--- window_models:

--- window_blend:
Step 1, cell 13 "W4 blend":
- load_runs(): fus = mean_logp over the final_s* runs, i.e. log of the mean probability. imu = mean_logp over the imu_s* runs.
- For test, each run's test_logp_raw is first renormalised with a log-softmax.
- blend(fus, imu, CFG['imu_w']=0.3) = log_softmax(fus + 0.3*imu), a product of experts.
- Results go to d['logp'] (OOF, 69326x19) and t['logp'] (test, 12234x19). fusion_logp and imu_logp are also kept.
- Window-level OOF macro F1: 0.7239 for 4F+2I (markdown, cell 3). A cell-13 comment gives 0.7194 fusion / 0.5995 IMU / 0.7253 blend.

Step 2, blend B (cell 22 tab_blend):
- log_softmax((1 - 0.8)*logp + 0.5*S3_logp + 0.3*T_logp), with TAB_TEST_CHOICE = (('S3',0.5),('T',0.3)).
- TEST_LOGP_B uses the S3 and T models trained on all OOF rows. OOF_LOGP_B uses the LOFO versions.
- Blend B followed by label propagation, L0 smoothing and calibration scores 0.8776 OOF (scoreboard row "+ tabular experts S3 and T").

Step 3, stage B (cell 36):
- B2 = tab_blend(logp, [(S3, 0.5), (adapted T, 0.3)]).

No weight is tuned in code; all weights are constants.
--- graph_and_propagation:
Cell 19 diagnostic, context_baseline:
- label_prop with CFG['lp']=(k=10, tau=0.1, use_p=0.5, alpha=0.9) and 20 iterations.
- Per subject, the similarity is S = cos(unit emb) + 0.5*sqrt(P)·sqrt(P). Keep the top-10 neighbours, set W = exp((S - max)/tau), symmetrise with max, normalise as D^-1/2 W D^-1/2, and iterate f = alpha*W f + (1-alpha) f0.
- Then link_smooth over the L0 links (alpha 0.5, 10 iterations, weights sigmoid(score)), then finish().

PLP (cell 20):
- The same label_prop applied to d['logp']/t['logp'] gives PLP_OOF/PLP_TEST, used only as L2 pair features.

Main graph stage (cell 26), Runner and graph_P:
- emb is d['emb'] / t['emb'], the frame-mean video embedding from final_s0, not a classifier output.
- Embedding smoothing first: E' = E + g * rownorm(Lw) @ E, where Lw is the link graph with weights sigmoid((score - b)/1) and link bias b=-2.0.
- SubjectKNN keeps the top K=40 per subject by cos(E') + use_p*sqrt(P0)·sqrt(P0), where P0 = exp(input blend).
- knn_W uses the top k=10 with tau=0.1, symmetrised by max and row-normalised (norm='row').
- label_prop_generic iterates 20 times: g = (1-bb)*A f + bb*(rownorm(Lw) f), with bb = beta*min(deg/2, 1) in 'abs' mode, then f = alpha*g + (1-alpha)*f0.
- Self-training rounds (r): Q = Sinkhorn-calibrate(P^2) (T=2.0), the new prior is P0^(1-w)*Q^w renormalised, and propagation repeats with the kNN graph still built on P0.
- Config string format: g{emb_g}/{abs|row}{beta}/b{link_b}/a{alpha}/r{rounds}w{w}[/t{tau}].
- TEST_CFGS (public, g=0.5): ['g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r3w0.5', 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5', 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.93/r2w0.7', 'g0.5/abs0.6/b-2.0/a0.93/r3w0.5', 'g0.5/abs0.8/b-2.0/a0.95/r2w0.7'].
- OUR_CFGS are the same 5 with g0.5 replaced by g1.5 ("kNN graph leaning on the links", +0.003 OOF). /t0.3 is appended when WEAR_WHITEN>0.
- graph_P returns the arithmetic mean of the 5 configs' pre-calibration probabilities.

Bagging (cells 32 and 34):
- bag_P runs graph_P once per matching (8 matchings) and takes the geometric mean.

Optional whitening (WEAR_WHITEN=0.5, off):
- Subject-centred embedding, PCA on the OOF tiles, each axis scaled by var^-0.5; kNN tau becomes 0.3.

No per-subject PC removal is done in this notebook beyond subject centring. The PCA in the tabular features (K_PCA=48) is for T and S3 only.
--- links:
L0 (cell 18, Hanh Tran / Briano):
- Candidates are the union of video top-k: tail3·head3 (k=40), last·first frame (15), mean·mean (15).
- 12 pair features, including IMU gap/extrapolation when the limb is the same.
- LGBMClassifier with 200 rounds, 4 negatives, balanced classes, trained on train_tiles.
- l0_assign: Hungarian assignment, links with log-odds < -1 dropped, each cycle broken at its weakest link.
- Per-fold OOF; the test scorer is trained on all recordings. Saved to links_L0.npz.
- OOF ref "(ours: 0.924 / 0.330 / 0.887)" for linked / exact / same-label. Used by S3 neighbor_block and the diagnostic.

L2 (cells 23-25):
- Ridge boundary predictors fit from Gram statistics of consecutive stride-10 tile pairs: video blocks [First, Head3, Tail3, Last, Mean] forward and backward, plus per limb-pair IMU ridges. RIDGE_LAM 0.1, IMU_LAM 1e-3, SHRINK 0.1; ridge r2 about 0.58.
- Candidates: 11 SOURCES (th 40, lf 15, mm 15, lfc 15, imu r8/c5, extlin r25/c10, lfz c30, ext2 25, dual 25), capped at KCAP=160 by n_src, plus the ridge-score top KNEW=20 per ridge model.
- 65 features: BASE49 (stored float16) + NEW16 ridge log-likelihoods. Four of them use the window probabilities: bc_w = Bhattacharyya(sqrt(exp(logp))), bc_lp = Bhattacharyya on PLP, maxp_i and maxp_j from PLP.
- Scorer: LightGBM cross_entropy (LK_PARAMS: lr 0.05, 63 leaves, min_data_in_leaf 100, 300 rounds), trained on OOF rows of the true successor plus LK_NEG=60 random negatives per tile. The code comment says 16, but LK_NEG is 60 here.
- Test log-odds are the mean over the 5 ridge variants. lk_assign (Hungarian, floor -50, cycle breaking) picks links; qnorm maps scores per subject to the QN_REF 1001-point quantile table.
- Per-subject arrays saved: pairs_{s}.npz (cand, valid, base, new) and testpairs_{s}.npz.
- Reported: linked 94.8%, exact successor 56.2%, same bout 93.7%; test linked "(ours 0.951)".

Two-tower matcher (cells 15-16, train_link.py):
- Tail and head towers: bidirectional GRU over the video plus a CNN over the IMU with emphasis on the edges. Score = scale*cos(g, h) + [same limb]*<gs, hs>.
- Contrastive softmax within 2 recordings x 512 tiles (two contiguous blocks of 256), random phase. 3000 steps, lr 1e-3, EMA 0.999, seeds 0/1/2.
- Held-out top-1 32% (cosine baseline 24%), top-40 94%.

Pair features from both boundary models (cell 28):
- Per seed, 8 two-tower features: row and column log-softmax in both directions, dominance in the row and column, log ranks.
- 7 SSL features from a per-subject ridge mapping first frame to last frame (lam 0.01), used forward and backward.
- 65 + 24 + 7 = 96 features total.

L3 (cell 30):
- LambdaRank, one query per tile (LK_RANK: ndcg, truncation 20, label_gain [0,1], deterministic, force_row_wise), 300 rounds, LK_SEEDS=3.
- Scores mapped back to log-odds by LogisticRegression(C=10) on every 7th training row (calibration()).
- OOF from LOFO scorers; test is the mean over 3 seeds and 5 ridge variants. Hungarian lk_assign, then qnorm.
- Exact successor 67.8%, linked 96.3%, same bout 94.7%. Feature gain shares: public 18%, two-tower 75%, SSL 7%.

Bagging (cell 32):
- BAG_K=8 matchings: the plain one plus 7 with Gumbel noise of scale BAG_TAU=0.3 on the candidate log-odds. Each passes the clean log-odds of the chosen link, quantile-normalised.
--- counts_constraint:
Per-subject Sinkhorn with 50 iterations.

Public version (cell 8, calibrate, inside finish):
- Applied after sharpening Q = P^(1/sharpen_T) = P^2.
- Each exercise column sums to per_ex=97 x sets (sets = TRAIN_SETS {0:2, 14:2} for train; 1 for test).
- Null gets max(n - 18*97*sets, null_min*n) with null_min=0.05. The remaining mass is split equally over the 18 exercises.

Learned count prior (cell 34):
- profile_features over the matrices [bagged graph P, exp(blend B)]. For each (subject, class 1..18) it computes the sorted margin log q_c - log max_other, read at ranks RK=[60,70,80,90,97,105,115,125,140] x sets.
- It adds sum q / sets, argmax count / sets, and the counts of margin > 0, > -1, > 1, plus n / sets.
- fit_counts: standardise, RidgeCV(alphas [1,3,10,30,100,300,1000]) fit only on targets with 55 < true < 150, predictions clipped to [70, 135].
- OOF counts are nested by fold. Test counts use a fit on all 22 subjects.
- count_targets: null = max(n - sum per-class counts, 0.05n); the per-class counts are rescaled to n - null.
- passes=2: pass 1 uses the fixed-97 decode. The graph is then re-decoded with the targets inside the self-training rounds (Runner.cal uses calibrate_targets), and the counts are re-estimated.
- finish_targets: sharpen ^2, then Sinkhorn to the targets.
- Mean count error 7.3 tiles vs 9.8 for a fixed 97 (scoreboard; section 10 quotes 7.5). The oracle with true counts gives 0.9362.
--- refiner:
Cell 38, "O7 boundary refiner along the chains".

Settings: REF_H=3, REF_THR=0.5, REF_ROUNDS=300. REF_PARAMS: binary LightGBM, lr 0.05, 31 leaves, min_data_in_leaf 50, feature_fraction 0.8, bagging 0.8, l2 1.

Candidate rows:
- For each of the 8 bagged matchings, find every link i->j whose final labels differ (fin = QB argmax).
- Take the tiles at chain offsets -2..0 on the i side (via prv) and +1..+3 on the j side (via succ).
- Each row asks whether that tile should take the other side's label.

37 features per row:
- offset, cur is null, other is null.
- Differences between cur and other in blend B2 (log), graph P (log), the window logp d['logp'], and calibrated Q (log).
- B[g,0] and lP[g,0], movement energy, video motion, and video-mean distances to the predecessor and successor.
- For chain neighbours at -2, -1, +1, +2: B diff, lP diff, energy diff, same sensor, posture distance when the sensor is the same, and whether fin[nb] == cur.

Training and decision:
- Target: y == other and y != fin. Trained LOFO by fold on the OOF chains; the test model is trained on all rows.
- A tile flips when its mean probability over K=8 matchings (absent counts as 0) exceeds 0.5; the highest-mean other label wins.

Results: changes 1,253 OOF tiles and 157 test tiles; OOF 0.9275 -> 0.9308. With the true order it would gain +0.016 to +0.018 (oracle).

--- plug_in_point:
Contract every option must satisfy:

1. OOF array. Shape (69326,19) of log-probabilities (or probabilities), in sim row order.
   - Build it from sim = np.load(ROOT/'sim_meta.npz'), which cell 10 loads as `sim`.
   - For row r: stem = recs[sim['rec'][r]]['stem'], where recs is the json in PROC/recordings.json, indexed in sorted inertial_feat CSV order.
   - start = sim['start'][r], in 50 Hz samples, always a multiple of 50. Video frames are start*3//5+8 .. +22.
   - limb = sim['sensor'][r], indexed into ['right_arm','right_leg','left_leg','left_arm'].
   - From our stride-25 4-limb OOF of shape (n_windows,4,19), take window (stem, start), i.e. k = start/25 if our index starts at 0 with no filtering, at our limb index for that sensor name.
   - Sanity check: our window label at that start equals sim['y'][r] (majority rule, ties to the lower class).
2. Test array. Shape (12234,19), in test_meta_data.csv row order, NOT sorted by id.
   - ids = np.load(PROC/'test_id.npy', allow_pickle=True).
   - ext_test = pd.DataFrame(our_test, index=our_ids).loc[ids].to_numpy().
   - Check that the sensor matches t['sensor'].
3. Folds. OOF semantics must be subject-held-out under the notebook's folds (sim['fold'], fold_of).
   - Several downstream learners are trained on OOF and applied to test: S3, the L2/L3 link scorers via bc_w/bc_lp/maxp, the count-prior ridge via Bp=exp(blend), the pseudo-label adaptation via QA_OOF, and the refiner via the B/lw features.
   - Our scene-cluster OOF must therefore not let a model see other tiles of the same subject; otherwise these stages will over-trust our column.
   - OOF on the train tiles is mandatory for every option. There is no test-only plug-in that stays consistent.
   - No weight is auto-tuned. Pick our weight by the printed OOF numbers: cell 13 window F1, the cell-22 "blend B nested OOF", and the final SCORES table in cell 38.

Option C, zero logic change (recommended first). Package our model as an extra fusion run.
- Create ROOT/runs/ext/ with:
  - oof_raw.npz: logp = log of our probabilities, (69326,19) float32, in sim order; y = sim['y']; grp = sim['sbj']; emb = any (69326,768) array, unused unless the run is FUSION[0].
  - test_logp_raw.npy: (12234,19), test_meta order.
  - test_emb.npy.
- Edit cell 12, last lines: FUSION = [RUNS_DIR / n for n in ('final_s0','final_s1','final_s2','final_s3','ext') if run_complete(n)].
- final_s0 must stay first because of the assert, and emb comes from FUSION[0].
- Cell 13 then takes the mean probability over 5 runs, which gives our model weight 1/5 inside fus before the 0.3*imu product. To raise the weight, list 'ext' several times, or add it to IMU with weight 0.3.
- W3 will not try to train 'ext', because it trains only FULL_RUNS.

Option A, explicit weight.
- In cell 13, after the d={...} and t={...} dicts are built (before `del _o,...`):
  d['logp'] = _lsm(d['logp'] + lam*EXT_OOF_LOGP)
  t['logp'] = _lsm(t['logp'] + lam*EXT_TEST_LOGP)
  where _lsm is a row log-softmax (same as blend()).
- Optionally also set d['fusion_logp']/t['fusion_logp'], which are S3 input columns 3x19. Keep the shapes so the cell-22 asserts (S3 533 and T 361 columns) still pass.
- Every later consumer picks this up automatically: cell 19 diagnostic; cell 20 PLP; cell 22 S3 features and tab_blend base weight 0.2; cell 24 L2 pair features bc_w/bc_lp/maxp_i/maxp_j, written into pairs_{s}.npz/testpairs_{s}.npz and therefore into L3 too; cell 26 public graph; cells 34 and 36 decodes; cell 38 refiner feature lw=d['logp'].
- The whole CPU pipeline reruns (about 3 h), with the GPU-outputs dataset still attached for runs/ and link*.

Option B, links untouched (decode-only effect). Add our model as an expert in tab_blend.
- Cell 22: TEST_LOGP_B = tab_blend(t['logp'], [(TAB_TEST['S3'],0.5),(TAB_TEST['T'],0.3),(EXT_TEST_LOGP,w)]), and OOF_LOGP_B the same with TAB_OOF and EXT_OOF_LOGP.
- Cell 36: B2_OOF and B2_TEST get the same extra (EXT, w) part.
- Caveat: tab_blend gives the window logp weight 1 - sum(w), so w=0.2 zeroes the notebook's window blend. Either lower S3/T or accept that.
- This changes the graph decode, count profiles, pseudo-labels and refiner, while L2/L3 links stay identical to the notebook's.

Cheapest experiment loop:
- Keep WEAR_ABLATION=1, because 0 crashes (see risks).
- Set WEAR_DUMP=1 to obtain B/P/Q and the 8 matchings for offline re-decoding.

Comments and docstrings say 16 negatives, but with the default 60 the public L2 LINK_MODEL and submission_public_pipeline.csv are not the original public pipeline. The '(ours: 1,173,696 ...)' row-count comment refers to 16 negatives.",
"Not reproducible. A Kaggle CPU rerun with identical GPU outputs gives 98.7% identical labels and LB 0.92620 vs 0.92990, and GPU retraining moves the LB by up to 0.005. Any fork must be compared on OOF, not against 0.92990.",
"The public LB band is about +/-0.004 to 0.005 (six link-scorer variants scored 0.92349 to 0.92990 with uncorrelated OOF). Gains of 0.0003 to 0.003 are invisible on the public LB, and the private split may rank differently.",
"Artifact detection only globs /kaggle/input/*/runs/final_s0/oof_raw.npz, one level deep. If Kaggle mounts the dataset deeper (e.g. /kaggle/input/datasets/<user>/<slug>/), ART is empty. Without a GPU the notebook then falls to CPU_RUNS (4 epochs x 100 steps) and scores far lower. Set WEAR_ARTIFACTS explicitly in a fork.",
"The cell-13 assert (_o['grp']==sim['sbj']) works only because the train subject ids are exactly 0..21 (grp holds the SB index). If ids changed, it would fail.",
"Hand-picked graph configs (TEST_CFGS 'ranked on all 5 folds') and the QN_REF quantile table come from full-OOF selection, so the 'nested' OOF is slightly optimistic. Per-fold spread is large (0.9177 to 0.9446), and one subject is worth about 0.002.",
"Plugging in our probabilities: our OOF uses scene-cluster folds, not subject folds. If a subject's tiles were scored by a model trained on the same subject's other scenes, our OOF is optimistic. S3, the L2/L3 link scorers (bc_w/bc_lp/maxp features), the count ridge, pseudo-labels and the refiner would then over-weight our column, and the decode on test would be miscalibrated.",
"The notebook's own oracle says better window models are a weak lever: fixing 30% of window errors (0.725 to 0.796 per tile) gives only +0.003. Expected gain from our 0.785-LB ensemble is therefore at most about 0.002 to 0.003, inside the LB noise.",
"The tile label is the majority label with ties going to the lower class index (null). The test labelling rule is unknown. With WEAR_CLEAN off, the unlabelled sbj_10/sbj_2 tails (1,531 tiles) and the 10 s lag of sbj_7 stay in training and evaluation.",
"CLEANUP deletes /tmp/wear at the end, so the intermediate pairs and links are lost unless WEAR_DUMP=1 or CLEANUP is disabled."
